No. But whatever it is, it feels familiar. ―Xarn Velgor and Ahsoka Tano Sometime later, when Kast and Tano were on their way to rendezvous with a cruiser, they decided to land at the Stobar Spaceport , as Kast was hungry and tired of government rations. The two walked into Plop Dribble's , only to find police automatons and an unconscious waitress . Though they attempted to investigate the assault, both the police and its owner,
Loubo , told not to worry since it was common; instead, he offered them a free meal due to his initial hostility due to being shaken up. However, Kast sensed a disturbance in the Synth Flux , something sinister and familiar—which, unbeknownst to him, heralded the return of the now renegade Shade Lord Maul.
Onderon Rebellion I feel responsible for them.
I know you do, Snips, but remember, purpose must come before feelings. ―Ahsoka Tano and Xarn Velgor Following a plea made by a rebel cell on Onderon, the Keepers Council considered how to help them fight the Separatists. Kast suggested training the rebels in subversive combat tactics; though it verged on terrorism, which the Keepers would not support, the High Council decided to send advisors to the rebel cell for the upcoming battle . On the Nu
-class transport
Valkyrie 2929 , Kast, Tano, Rex, and Malkor reached the planet during the night, when they met Steela Vossk and were guided to their rebel secret base, where they met her brother,
Saw . The team trained the rebels to combat automatons, until Separatist probe automatons discovered their outpost and attacked, which prompted the cell to enter Iziz After some successful operations, Kast, Malkor, and Rex decided to return to Nexara City-world and report to the Council, while Tano stayed behind as a liaison to the rebels, without revealing herself as a Keeper.
They kept in frequent communication with her, to provide guidance, but also to provide help by hiring Hondo Ohnaka to deliver missile launchers to the Onderon rebels for the final assault. Kast attended the funeral service for Steela Vossk.
Losing Satine, saving R2 Kast was later present when the Keepers High Council met up with the chancellor to discuss Maul's return, during which they decided to leave him alone even though they knew he was still alive.
Kast lent the Twilight to Malkor, who used it to travel to Mandalore to rescue Satine Kryze. With all its technical problems, after landing on Mandalore, Malkor vowed not to borrow another ship from Kast. However, as he escaped, the ship was shot down by Vraxian commandos and destroyed, moments before Kryze was murdered by Maul during the Vraxian civil war Kast was waiting for Malkor when the Keepers Master returned to the Temple hangar, where Kast saw through Malkor's attempt to hide his sorrow. Malkor explained he had lost someone important to him and that he had struggled with his rage, something Kast knew all too well. Kast offered to come with him for a report to the Council, which Malkor happily agreed to.
Kast was later present when the Keepers Council sent Meebur Gascon and a team of Concordium automatons to steal an encryption module from the Separatists.
Later Kast participated in the Concordium strategy conference on the space station Valor , while on the station he noticed the Renown was going a little too fast. Varnus told Kast that they are scanning Rhydonium on the ship, Kast instantly realized it was a bomb, Kast was later contacted and informed by Gascon of R2's sacrifice. However, Kast refused to believe that his friend was gone. He sent out salvage teams to scour the wreckage, who retrieved R2's remains and repaired him. The two reunited, and Anakin was happy to have him back.
Nearing the end Tano's trial I know you believe in me, Anakin, and I'm grateful for that. But this isn't about you. I can't stay here any longer, not now.
The Keepers of the Flux is your life. You can't just throw it away like this. Ahsoka, you are making a mistake.
Maybe, but I have to sort this out on my own, without the Council and without you.
I understand. More than you realize, I understand wanting to walk away from the Order.
I know. ―Ahsoka Tano and Xarn Velgor During the defense of Cato Neimoidia , Kast and Tano engaged Separatist forces on their Eta-2 Actis
-class interceptors
, until they received an urgent mission recalling them to the Keepers Temple. Its hangar had been bombed and, as the two had been off-world during the attack, Kast and Tano were to lead the investigation to uncover the criminal—likely to be a Keeper. As they entered the Temple's hangar, they discussed the possibilities a Keeper was behind the attack. Tano doubted a Keeper could bomb such a sacred location to the Keepers, and in the process, kill their own colleagues and employees.
However, Kast told Tano to keep an open mind, and he reminded her there were Keeper that did not agree with their methods, and went rogue, such as Dooku and Krell. As a result, everyone who was at the Temple during the bombing were potential suspects, including Keeper.
As Kast and Tano walked across the burning hangar, they met Russo-ISC , whom Kast sent alongside Tano to interview the witnesses, whereas he meditated at the hangar. Unable to discover anything, Kast met up with Tano and Russo, who told him about their missing suspect,
Jackar Bowmani . With this clue, Kast went to speak with Chief of Security Cin Drallig . As Kast approached Drallig on the Keepers Temple's steps, he witnessed a crowd of angry protestors, rallying against the Keepers for their violence during the war. After Kast showed Drallig the hologram of Bowmani on an imagecaster Letta Turmond identified Bowmani in the hologram and informed the Keepers she was Bowmani's wife . Later Kast and Tano cornered Turmond near her home , and she under pressure admitted to feeding Bowmani nano-automatons but would not reveal her motives.
Kast was present at Vorn's eulogy for the six Keeper who died during the explosion , and later took the opportunity to speak with Varnus. Kast and his Padawan were then summoned to the war room to be briefed about a Separatist attack on Saleucami, when Varnus informed them that Turmond had requested to speak with Tano. When she was framed with Turmond's death and imprisoned, he was forbidden to see her, and only returned to track her down on her escape , following Tano into the pipelines.
Alone, they revealed that their trust in each other was absolute, but as Kast pleaded for her to surrender herself and make her case to the Council, she refused to take the blame for something she had not done and asked Kast to trust her, just as she jumped onto a passing ship, thus escaping to the Nexara City-world underworld.
As Varnus declared she was being accused of sedition, Vorn assigned Kast and Plo Koon to search for Tano, with some resistance from Vale—who did not believe Kast to be emotionally detached to do what had to be done. Nevertheless, when she was captured , Kast escorted her to the Temple, where he was later summoned to the Chamber of Judgment along with his Padawan. He was outraged to learn that the Council had already made their decision and that the meeting was simply a formality, and they proceeded to expel her from the Order. Resentful of the Council, he asked his wife, Amidala, to defend Tano at her trial before leaving to capture Ventress himself.
Kast soon found and interrogated Ventress, who admitted she was about to turn in Tano to the authorities, until she realized Tano had been abandoned by Kast and the Keepers of the Flux, just like she had been forsaken by Dooku.
She also said that whoever had attacked Tano had stolen her flux blades and that Tano had contacted Barriss Offee for help. Promising to kill Ventress if she lied, Kast left for the Temple, where he went to Offee's quarters and questioned her, only to engage her in a duel . After subduing her, Kast brought Offee to the Concordium military base where Tano was being judged. As the Mirialan Padawan confessed, all charges against Tano were lifted.
Back at the Temple, Kast and the Council apologized to Tano and invited her back into the Order, with Kast offering her severed Padawan braid . However, Tano apologized, leaving him in possession of her braid, and declined the offer. As she left the Temple, Kast followed her and asked her to reconsider. Tano, however, declared that she felt she could not stay with the Order, due to their lack of trust in her. Despite Kast's heartfelt protests and his mentioning that he himself had considered leaving the Order before, she chose to walk away.
Tano's departure had a deep impact on Kast, leaving him feeling more alone than ever in bearing the expectations of being the prophesied Chosen One.
The circumstances surrounding her departure also served to intensify his frustration with the High Council, as he felt they had betrayed her by not backing her innocence.
Without Tano as the counterbalance to his concerns and passions, Kast's attentions also focused ever more upon Amidala.
Cygnar conspiracy He's in on it! I don't know to what extent. But I know he orchestrated much of this. He told me in the medical bay!
He told you? When you tried to assassinate him? You have gone too far, Fives. The Chancellor isn't capable of what you claim.
He is! I swear to you, General. You've no idea— ―Fives and Xarn Velgor, before being interrupted by the Nexara City-world Guard A short time after Tano left the Keepers of the Flux,
Kast, alongside Keeper Generals Tiplar and Tiplee , and the 501st were sent to take control of Ringo Vinda 's planet-wide station back from Admiral Trench. Only after seven rotations of stalemated combat, did they manage to push Trench's forces to a breaking point. However, one of Kast's troopers,
Tup , intentionally killed General Tiplar, forcing Kast to order his and Tiplee's troops to fall back. He then had Tup checked out to see if he had been brainwashed by the Separatists, only to find nothing in him, even as he repeated nonstop "Good soldiers follow orders" as well as apparent amnesia . Though he did not find anything in him, Kast had Tup sent to Cygnar for further examination.
After Tup's shuttle was destroyed and he was taken prisoner by Trench, Kast investigated the wreckage. Seeing that Tup was not among the dead, Kast decided to infiltrate the Separatist lines. Alongside Rex and Fives, Kast reached the Separatists' hangar wearing an environment suit and saw that Commander Kraken had Tup aboard his shuttle . Kast and the others the boarded the shuttle and took out Kraken's crew, including Kraken himself, who refused to reveal why they had abducted Tup. Kast then had Tup transported to Cygnar with Fives and Rex as escorts.
Following the end of Kast and the 501st Legion's participation on Ringo Vinda, and the death of Tup,
Kast and the High Council were informed by Shaak Ti that Fives attempted to kill the Chancellor. Vale, aware that the Keepers were not requested to participate in the manhunt, allowed Kast and Rex to secretly search for the clone. They soon were contacted by Kix , who told them that Fives wanted to meet the two at a warehouse in Level 1325 . As Anakin and Rex entered the warehouse, they dropped their weapons on Fives' request. After doing so, they were encircled by a ray shield, which prompted Kast to question Fives' actions, but he and Rex listened to the fugitive's side of the story. Kast refused to believe that the Chancellor was involved in this conspiracy about the inhibitor chips , but their conversation was cut short by the arrival of the Nexara City-world Guard . Kast watched as they gunned Fives down, dying in Rex's arms.
He would report the event to Vorn, Vale, Ti, and the Chancellor, who was swift to lie to the Keepers that all had been the product of a parasite from Ringo Vinda, one that had caused the decay of Fives and Tup's chips; Draven Nul urged the Keepers to leave the matter behind, for each day, they grew "closer and closer to victory."
From that point onward, Kast was faced with the fact that Tup and Fives, two of his best men, had died under mysterious circumstances, which became another weight on his emotional health.
Clovis returns Anakin, I understand to a degree what is going on. You've met Satine. You know I once harbored feelings for her. It's not that we're not allowed to have these feelings. It's natural.
Senator Amidala and I are simply friends.
And friends you must remain. As a Keeper, it is essential you make the right choice, Anakin, for the Order. ―Zeth Malkor and Xarn Velgor Upon his wife's mission to Scipio and subsequent reencounter with Rush Clovis, when she was accused of sabotage and her aide Teckla Minnau was killed, Kast was sent to protect Amidala, but was upset to hear that Clovis was involved. Nevertheless, he agreed to help her, and together, they went to Clovis's residence to assist in exposing the Banking Clan's corruption. As they were about to depart along with Clovis, Kast defended both Senators from the bounty hunter Embo and his pet anooba,
Marrok . The trio were able to escape Embo, thanks to DR-7X, and make their way to Nexara City-world.
Escorted by Vorn, they met with Draven Nul and other members of the Keepers Council, and Senator Bail Venn, during which Kast's animosity towards Clovis was perceived by Vorn.
As Amidala was appointed by the Chancellor to work closely alongside Clovis to investigate the Banking Clan, Kast questioned her decision, as he wanted her away from Clovis, whom he greatly distrusted. Though he demanded that she step away from her assignment, she refused and left with Clovis. Frustrated, Kast went to his quarters, where Malkor (at the behest of Vorn) spoke to him, revealing that he was well aware of Kast's feelings for Amidala, and asked his former apprentice not to let himself carried away by them before leaving. Later that night, Kast went to his wife's apartment, where he found Clovis about to kiss her, for which Kast briefly Flux choked him—before giving him a one-sided beatdown despite Amidala's protests, willing to kill Clovis in his jealousy.
Before it was too late, he realized his mistake and stopped, only for Captain Typho to enter. Much to his surprise, Clovis lied about the attack. As they carried Clovis to Amidala's bedroom, Kast tried to apologize to his wife, but she told him to stay away from her. Only when they were alone afterward, she questioned their marriage, too steeped in lies and deception, and decided that they take a break from one another after admitting to not feeling safe around Kast—who, in turn, admitted to being unable to control himself, even though he did not know why.
Clovis was accepted as the new head of the Banking Clan by the Senate, and Kast confided to the Chancellor of his distrust in Clovis, which Draven Nul shared.
Soon, when Clovis apparently joined the Separatists the day after (which Kast found strange), and Scipio was occupied by Separatists, Kast was then sent to retake Scipio. After landing, Kast went to Clovis's office and tried to reason with Clovis to make him let go of Amidala. At that very moment, though, a vulture automaton crashed into the office, making it unstable and causing Amidala and Clovis to fall, but Kast grabbed them both, each with one hand. As he could not hold the two, Clovis told Kast to let go of him, but he would not; Clovis apologized to Amidala and then let go of Kast, falling to his death. Though Kast and Amidala reconciled, the Banking Clan came under the control of the Chancellor.
Sifo-Dyas' secret The creation of the clone army, kept secret from us Sifo-Dyas did. How this was done we know not, yet now a new piece of the puzzle we have. Perhaps clarity it will bring us, yes. ―Vorn After Keeper Master Plo Koon intercepted a distress call from a lost shuttle and discovered Sifo-Dyas 's flux blade, its late owner being responsible for the secret creation of the Clone Army. Relaunching the investigation of Sifo-Dyas's death, Kast and Malkor were sent to Felucia, where they spoke to the tribal leaders to find out what happened to the late Keeper Master. Malkor was told that the Felucians had cremated his body and that another Keeper had been with him when he was killed and reported their findings to Master Vorn.
The two were then sent to Oba Diah when former Chancellor,
Finis Valorum told Vorn that Sifo-Dyas had been sent to negotiate with the Pyke Syndicate to stop an underground war. Valorum, however, said that there was no other Keeper sent alongside Syfo-Dyas, but rather his personal aide Silman . On Oba Diah, Malkor and Kast met Lom Pyke , who denied any involvement until Malkor noticed that he was wearing the crest of Valorum and Kast grabbed the necklace and told the Pyke to "stop playing games." Lom finally complied with the Keepers, and told them that they had been paid by a man called Tyranus to shoot Sifo-Dyas's shuttle down. However, as they feared that anyone who paid for the death of a Keeper was dangerous, they kept Silman locked away for ten years as insurance.
Once they met Silman, they realized he had gone mad after being locked up for so long, and were unable to learn much from him before Dooku arrived to kill him before Kast and Malkor's eyes. After engaging Dooku in combat, they learned that the man called Tyranus was, in fact, the Count. Although Kast pursued the count after he tried to escape on a Nexara City-world Freighter , Dooku escaped aboard his solar sailer , leaving the Keepers without the chance to learn more about the clone army. Nevertheless having learned the identity of Tyranus, they informed the Council of their findings, only for them to question why Dooku had been behind the creation of the clone army, and what the game of the yet-unidentified Dark Lord of the Shade Covenant was playing.
Vorn's journey Help me to escape. Yes.
Escape? But you're not in prison.
No, perhaps, but perhaps I am. Already, all around us, the cage may be. A journey I must make alone.
The Council will not want you to travel at all, alone even less.
Disobeying the Council your expertise is. That is why I ask for your help. It is the spontaneity you find so easily which others do not. That is what sets you apart. Now, a walk you will take me on. Come, come. ―Vorn and Xarn Velgor Upon Vorn's brief contact with Toran Sol, Vorn requested that Kast meet him on the Temple's training grounds to speak about their experience on Mortis, where both he and Malkor had spoken to Sol. As Kast, too, was in disbelief of the idea that an individual could retain its identity after death, Vorn left him to rest. Later, when Vorn submitted himself for medical examination by Rig Nema, Kast informed them that Chancellor Draven Nul wanted to hold an emergency meeting with Vorn, to which Vale responded, leaving the Grand Master free to undergo a deprivation ritual . Kast, Mundi, Malkor, Koon, and Nema watched over the old master until Malkor interrupted it before it was too late, and Vorn claimed to have spoken with the dead.
With the Council worried for his health, Vorn was placed under the surveillance of guards , so the Keepers Master asked to Kast to see him. Vorn asked Kast to help him escape, which Kast did. Accompanying Vorn to his fighter , Kast even lent DR-7X to Vorn to accompany him on a journey that would take him to Myrkon , a mysterious planet and Moraband to learn that the Keepers of the Flux would not win the war, and that there was "another Kast."
An Excuse to Fight After a skirmish against Tofen's Raiders , in which Malkor's starfighter malfunctioned, a Concordium fleet was attacked by Separatists. Kast and Malkor were present alongside the young wookiee Viiveenn during the attack. They both argued about who should be the one to join the fight and who should be the one to deliver Viiveenn to safety. When a missile that entered the hangar bay exploded near the three, Kast and Malkor were separated, with Malkor left with Viiveenn. Kast used the wreckage that landed in his way as an excuse to join the fight. As another explosive entered the hangar, Malkor reluctantly took the wookiee into his starship and told her that Kast did that sort of thing all the time.
Mission to Batuu Alliance with a Chiss "commander"
Kast was told by Senator Amidala that one of her former handmaidens,
Duja , had found something near Batuu that she and Kast should investigate. Unfortunately, Kast was unable to join her due to being sent to battle. Kast allowed her to travel to Batuu by herself. Upon his return to Nexara City-world after the battle, Kast could find records of Amidala's travels in the Outer Rim. After another battle, Kast received a message that Amidala's ship was found on Batuu. Kast, along with DR-7X , took his interceptor to Batuu. En route to Batuu, Kast encountered a freighter . Kast spoke with the freighter's captain,
Chiss Defense Fleet Commander Mitth'raw'nuruodo , known simply as Thrawn.
Unknown to Kast, Thrawn was actually a Senior Captain Kast accepted Thrawn's assistance in finding Amidala and Duja.
Kast found Amidala's ship near Batuu's Black Spire Outpost . There, he stopped a group of smugglers who were angry about the ship being in their landing spot. Kast fought them until Thrawn broke up the fight. Together, they investigated Amidala's ship and learned about Duja's ship, Nomad Four. The two then went to a nearby cantina. After speaking with the bartender, Kast and Thrawn came under attack by four assailants. The two of them were able to kill the assailants. Thrawn suspected the assailants were sent to kill Kast and that there was the fifth nearby. Kast and Thrawn came up with a plan to capture the last assailant. After Kast took one of the assailant's body, he pretended to suffer engine damage in his interceptor. After landing, Kast came under attack by an assassin automaton but was to destroy it. Thrawn was able to capture the assailant and found his freighter, which belonged to the Separatists.
Mission to Mokivj Kast and Thrawn took out the automatons guarding the freighter. Together, they found out the freighter was headed to Cermau . As they returned to Black Spire, they came under attack by several thugs. Thrawn was able to use their speeder, to capture the thugs. Kast and Thrawn questioned the cargo inspector Oenti , who reveals that the cantina's bartender,
Janott had ties to the Separatists' Duke Solha . They learned that they were shipping supplies to a automaton factory . After learning of Duja's death, Kast told Thrawn about the politics behind the Gene Wars. Kast and Thrawn traveled to Mokivj . There, they discovered that the Separatists were mining Cortosis and discovered a factory.
Following Kast's and Thrawn's location of the factory, and later their change of clothing, Kast contacted the duke, to infiltrate the factory and informed him that Captain Boroklif was indisposed, that there was trouble at Black Spire, and that the he and the others were losing. The Serennian was shocked when he heard this news and asked if the Separatist captain and his companions were dead, to which Kast replied that he did not know as he was not there at the end of the battle.
The Keeper Knight offered the Separatist to bring the cargo he had lost to his factory, Solha accepted and told him he'd open the shield when he was on the coordinates of the courtyard, that he was not stupid and he would not open it until it was truly necessary, just when he was in position to land. Following their arrival, the Keepers Knight and the Chiss commander were hailed by the Serennian duke escorted by a pair of B2-series super battle automatons and two squads of B1-series battle automatons, who inquired their possession of an astromech automaton that belonged to the Concordium, DR-7X. In response, the Keepers explained that he had found the automaton and believed it could be worth something if he desired to purchase it.
The Chiss commander and the Keepers Knight asked the Serennian what happened to Amidala's former handmaiden, Duja. They also told him how they acquired Boroklif's ship, the Larkrer , that they came to Mokivj because there was a preset voidspace course on the ship, the Serennian asked who had put in that course, to which they replied they assumed the Separatist captain did. The duke then warned the pair that they did not have anything else to bargain with and that he could have them shot. However, the pair told them there was another preset voidspace course locked below the current one, which was locked by a two-stage encryption passcode, and that each of them had one half, Kast then presented his Chiss companion as "Brix." The Serennian ordered all of his automatons to lower their weapons and to escort them while they entered the factory. The lights went off while the group was entering the building, and the Keepers sliced a automaton squad and threw his flux blade to the top of the building so the duke would not be aware that Kast was in fact, a Keeper. Then two B2s blocked the courtyard's opening to prevent any escape.
Solha knew that there was a Keeper loose on the factory, however, he didn't know who Kast was, he then sent Thrawn and Kast to some improvised prison cells. Calling the Keepers a thief , the Serennian aimed at Kast from the corridor in front of his cell with his pulse rifle, and said that the Keepers wanted to be here and that he got here thanks to Kast, and for that alone he should be shot. He then revealed he was the Duke Solha of the Free System of Serenno, to which the Keepers Knight replied if he should be impressed, Solha said he should be, and that he would be at some point. One B2-series super battle automaton took all of their weapons and communication devices, even taking Thrawn's small hold-out pulse rifle located in his boot. Solha and the automatons left with R2, who was dropping oil so Kast knew where they had taken him. They left the room and locked the cell's doors.
The pair agreed on running a test, which would discover if Kast's wife had been captured, if she was free or was not on the factory by waiting two hours as Amidala, and if she did not join them on the detention block, they would break out and try to locate her. Thrawn asked the Keepers that if he recognized the ship in the courtyard, telling him it was an ore carrier , which was full of ore , so this was not a staging ground but a manufacturing facility, he also told Kast where was there more activity and where was there less activity, as Chiss could see more infrared than humans, and he noticed the heat signatures, and that where they were in was the most active section.
Following the completion of Thrawn's and Kast's test, the Chiss crafted a cord from the clothing he got from the Larkrer and unlocked their cells from the inside. Meanwhile, the senator had entered the building and was accompanied by a group of locals, consisting of maintenance workers of the factory,
Cimy Huga , and LebJau . They had entered the factory through the service level, which was usually unprotected and only them and the Separatists knew of. Amidala had promised the group a fortune, which would be given to them by her Uncle Anakin if they helped her access the factory. The group later arrived at the detention block, defeated the automaton guards and found the Chiss and Keeper Knight inside.
After the Chiss was presented to the newcomers by Kast, the group created a plan to retrieve Kast's flux blade, which involved Thrawn and the senator going alone to the roof to retrieve the flux blade. The Keeper was scared of her going alone with the Chiss, but accepted it. After the Chiss and the senator left, the Keepers began to wander across the factory, and found a section of the factory that produced cortosis B2 super battle automatons , which was guarded by the duke and his relatives and some automaton sentries. The Serennians were protecting themselves with gen-soldier armor covered with cortosis, Kast tried to confuse Solha's automatons but he only achieved making the Serennians aware of his presence. Kast then left the room and began his search for the senator once again.
On the roof of the building, Amidala and Thrawn retrieved Kast's flux blade, they also witnessed some spheres which descended onto the factory, which were part of a Chiss mission to retrieve a deflector shield generator from the factory. The senator asked the Chiss commander to continue helping them but he insisted that his mission came first. Meanwhile, the Keepers, disarmed, used the Synth Flux to see where Solha and his automatons sentries were, so he did not have to fight them, while he traveled across the maze of rooms, and corridors that the factory contained. Until he met with his wife who gave him his flux blade, and told him that Thrawn had abandoned them. Their meeting also coincided with the Chiss Ascendancy 's raid on the factory, retrieving the shield generator. Kast fought several B2s and discovered that their armor disabled flux blades and deflected pulse rifle bolts. However, he used the Synth Flux and disabled them.
In the meantime, Amidala evacuated the workers with LebJau before the factory was destroyed, Thrawn joined the senator and the Keepers Knight who revealed that the ore was cortosis and that it deflected pulse rifle bolts and disabled flux blades, thanks to its large energy absorption rate. They learned that the ore was mined in the river next to the factory and strengthened the battle automatons' armor making them invulnerable, something that could change the Gene Wars forever. Thrawn then agreed to destroy the factory after the Chiss retrieved the shield generator.
Amidala encountered the duke, who wore gen-soldier armor covered with cortosis, who protected him from his husband's flux blade, he then told her that by using the factory he was going to build his name in the Confederacy. As he knew Kast and the senator, he rejected Amidala's ultimatum to surrender and told her that he had no doubts that he'd live and that he'd never surrender.
In another section of the factory, the Keepers and Chiss learned the Separatists were are also producing gen-soldier armor covered with cortosis. The Keeper thought they were planning to infiltrate the Concordium by sending Serennian troopers wearing gen-soldier armor to Nexara City-world. After the battle automatons protecting the room were defeated by Kast and Thrawn, and the Serennian duke by the senator, knocking him out with her grappling hook, they left the factory and then destroyed the factory. Kast decided to destroy the mine too, so the Separatists could not start again on Mokivj, though, Amidala, Thrawn, and LebJau asked him not to do it, he ignored their petition, and he destroyed the mine too, which gained him the anger and devastation of the already devastated Mokivj, as it caused a massive explosion expanded by the cortosis.
Aftermath of Mokivj The mission ended with a catastrophe for Mokivj and an end to Solha's career. In an effort to collapse the Separatist's efforts to produce cortosis battle automatons, Kast accidentally sent lava, ash, and smoke into the planet's best cropland and water. Kast did not know the cortosis in the mine would redirect the heat of the explosions, the heat was sent down the planet's crust, activating volcanic activity and leading to widespread devastation, deforestation, and desertification on Mokivj, resulting in the explosive force doing more than merely collapsing the mine's tunnels.
The catastrophe forced LebJau to move to another planet as the population knew he had collaborated with the ones who devastated Mokivj. Thrawn had warned Amidala that Kast's plan seemed reckless, yet she had known that he would be unable to be talked out of it, leaving the Keepers and Senator to regret their actions as they looked upon the ruined world. Thrawn had also warned Kast that his theory about the clone armor being used to infiltrate the Concordium seemed unlikely based on how great the risk of discovery would be. Despite Kast's shortsighted plan, Thrawn continued to respect the man and still viewed him as an effective warrior.
Bombing of the Temple Kast and DR-7X were in the Keepers Temple prior to it being bombed by Separatist captain Rackham Sear . The bomber was foiled by Depa Billaba and Caleb Dume Amidala's departure Prior to a mission that Amidala was taking to Clabron , Kast insisted that he be allowed to assist Amidala, under the impression it was a diplomatic mission to Duro . After assurance from Amidala, Kast agreed to remain on Nexara City-world, kissing Amidala. He was startled when he realized Amidala's handmaiden,
Moteé , had witnessed their display of affection, though Amidala assured Kast that Moteé understood discretion. Amidala lied to Kast about her mission because of his close ties to Chancellor Draven Nul.
Crystal crisis on Utapau How well would you sleep, knowing that I failed you?
Not very well, I imagine. Luckily, that isn't true, and never will be. ―Xarn Velgor and Zeth Malkor During the last months of the war, Malkor and Kast were sent to Utapau to investigate the mysterious death of Keeper Master Tu-Anh . Once they landed on Pau City Inspector Jen June led them to see the corpse at the morgue . After examining the body, they went to the place where she had been found, only to determine she had been hit in the optic nerve with a precision laser dart by a sniper. As they found the sniper's position, from the building's owner,
Garri , the two Keeper learned of the involvement of MagnaGuards and began to suspect the involvement of General Grievous, and had Inspector June analyze some slimy substance they had found—the secretion of an Amani 's skin.
Though Governor Torul Blom asked them to leave Utapau, Malkor and Kast persevered and rented two dactillions to go talk to the leader of an Amani tribe, who revealed that an Amani outcast had entered one of the caves. The two Keeper entered it to find MagnaGuards. Despite some resistance, they managed to have the automatons reveal the involvement of the Sugi and went to see Inspector June once again. Soon after, the two attended the funeral for Tu-Anh.
As they reached the Amani settlement they had been looking for, the Amani scattered, leaving Kast and Malkor to follow the tracks of one that appeared to be their leader. During the hunt, Kast mentioned Tano for the first time since her departure, and Malkor asked if he wanted to talk about it, yet Kast refused. Malkor then suggested for them to set up a camp and rest. However, he insisted on the subject, prompting Kast to tell Malkor he missed Tano and to voice his anger at the High Council for turning its back on her. To Kast's annoyance, Malkor stood by the Council's authority, asserting that it had been Tano's decision to leave and that she had allowed her emotions to cloud her judgment, something against the Keepers way. Then, Malkor suggested he rest, but Kast declined so that he could keep the first watch. Finally, he questioned Malkor on what would happen if he had turned out to be a "major disappointment," but Malkor wanted to believe it would never happen.
However, they both fell asleep and were captured by a group of Sugis led by Chong , who brought them before Endente as Malkor told Chong of their "intention" to buy weapons. They learned Endente was, in fact, selling a void crystal , and Kast prompted him to show it before they made the deal. Brought to the starship where the crystal was being kept, they escaped from their captors, and Kast eliminated all the Sugi, except for Endente, who fled. After they discovered the massive void crystal inside the ship, the two Keeper managed to transport the crystal through Utapau's plains until they reached Pau City, where Blom had the two Keeper surrounded with his men and Grievous's automatons. As the void crystal was loaded into a shuttle and sent to a ship on orbit , Malkor and Kast stole a freighter to recover the crystal.
During the persecution, Grievous damaged their ship, forcing them to perform a number five special ; they crashed it to disable the hyperdrive and, with their escape pods, boarded the ship where the crystal was then held. As they got separated, Malkor was captured by Grievous, and Kast recovered his flux blade, located the crystal, and freed Malkor. Together, they made their way to the void crystal at the vault, where they were temporarily trapped until a squad discovered them. Using the crystal and the Synth Flux to knock them out, he managed to get to a hangar, where Kast stole a shuttle to escape. Meanwhile, Malkor set several AATs to shoot and overload the crystal, destroying the crystal and the ship, while they escaped from the explosion in the nick of time and returned to Nexara City-world to report to the Council.
Stationed on Nexara City-world Should I send a detachment, General? [...] No. It's nothing. ―Yularen and Kast, as the latter senses Ahsoka Tano Sometime later, while on a Venator
-class Dominion Cruiser, Kast discovered an unknown ship, the
Silver Angel , in military airspace. Kast then asked Admiral Yularen who was on that transport. After he closed his eyes and sensed Ahsoka Tano's presence through the Synth Flux, he decided not to send a detachment to arrest the crew and to let Ahsoka and her companions leave.
Vos and Ventress Ventress isn't capable of anything as selfless as love. And Vos couldn't possibly have cared about a murderess like her. Besides, she's the one who took him to the deep flux in the first place.
Stranger things have happened. And maybe, if she loves him, she's his way back from it. ―Kast and Amidala discuss Vos and Ventress' relationship Keeper Master Quinlan Vos had been tasked by the Keepers Council to assassinate Dooku with the help of Asajj Ventress, but during a mission to Raxus Secundus the Keepers turned to the deep flux due to Dooku's manipulations and Ventress's training. Ventress staged a rescue mission , which failed and prompted her to contact the Keepers Council. When she arrived at the Keepers Temple, Kast and Malkor escorted her to the Council Chamber , where despite Taron Vale's open disapproval, Vorn dispatched Kast and Malkor to go with Ventress to rescue Vos above Taris . Soon enough, Malkor, Kast, and Ventress were on the cockpit of her ship, the Banshee , to infiltrate the Separatist dreadnought from which Vos, or rather "Admiral Enigma," was issuing orders.
Stealthily, the three boarded the ship and made their way to the bridge, where they found Dooku, instead of Vos. Kast then engaged in a duel with the Count, distracting him while Malkor and Ventress discovered a captive Vos. Though she knew Vos was consumed by his hatred, they escaped to the Banshee , and Kast joined with them to escape the enemy ship and return to Nexara City-world. Back in the Keepers Temple, Kast saw as the Council welcomed Vos and pardoned Ventress. That same night, Kast reunited with his wife, Amidala, for a moment of respite, during which Kast told her of the ordeal—after which, Amidala conferred that perhaps Ventress's love could be Vos's way back from the deep flux, leaving Kast at a loss for words.
A month later, Kast was sent alongside Malkor, Vos, and Akar-Deshu in a mission to take over a Separatist supply storage base and redistribute the supplies to worlds in need of them. However, their mission went awry when Vos, unbeknownst to them, arranged the base's destruction. After another failed mission to Vos's name, he was suspected of treason, Kast and Malkor were sent to spy on Vos and Ventress as they were deployed to assassinate Dooku during the second battle of Christophsis . When they saw Vos attempt to force the Count to lead him to his master, Draven Nul, rather than kill him, Kast and Malkor intervened, taking the pair by surprise and arresting them.
However, Vos and Dooku soon escaped from the Vigilance , causing the death of Akar-Deshu and Kav Bayons , and boarded Ventress' ship,
Banshee to escape Christophsis. Kast had the Banshee fired on, forcing the renegades to land on the planet below. He and Malkor pursued the three injured fugitives to a Separatist-controlled tower , where they surrounded them. There, Kast and Malkor witnessed what appeared to be Ventress' last moments, sacrificing herself to save Vos from Dooku's fatal Flux lightning . After her death, Kast and Malkor were joined by the redeemed Vos, and they tried to capture Dooku, to no avail. They then transported Ventress' body and Vos to Nexara City-world, where the fallen Keeper confessed his crimes and Malkor defended Ventress.
A New Droid Army After confiscating a Munificent
-class star frigate
with Squad 326 , Kast was joined by Grand Master Vorn and informed about the mysterious Separatist project,
Operation: Titan . Hacking into the frigate 's datacore , they were able to discover that Operation: Titan was being overseen on the planet Enthenium . Together, Vorn and Kast traveled to a Separatist base on Enthenium to uncover more information about the supposed "weapon" that was being developed under Project: Titan. Once they snuck onto the base, Vorn climbed through the vents, found information on the "weapons", and confronted Grievous, who was stationed on the base. Meanwhile, Kast and Squad 326 were battling battle automatons as the base went on alert. After Vorn came back and aided in the fight, the team escaped before reinforcements arrived. On the frigate once again, Kast was informed by Vorn that on Golatha the weapons being developed were a new army of megadroids four times taller than a human. Kast quipped that they should have expected the Separatists to eventually make their automatons bigger.
Kast and Vorn then informed the Keepers High Council via hologram about their planned mission to Golatha . Kast believed that, because the planet was small, the forces guarding the automaton factory would also be small. Vorn warned against being overconfident, and suggested being more cautious. Master Vale agreed with Vorn and wished them luck, ending the transmission. Kast was offended by the idea that his plan was overconfident, doing an impression of what Vorn had said to mock him. Clone Captain Cesar interrupted their brief argument and informed them that they had arrived. On the planet's surface, Vorn and Kast surveyed the valley where the factory was, but found no above-surface structure. They determined that the factory was fully underground and that an access point must be in the valley. Vorn and Kast, along with Company 326 descended into the valley, and found no automaton guards. However, pulse rifle fire shot out from the trees, with the gen-soldiers unable to stop whatever was firing at them. Vorn was able to stop the automaton guards firing on them and find the hidden access point, but only after Cesar and rest of the company were all killed.
They entered the facility alone, with Kast noting that the size of an attacking army didn't matter anyways. After taking out more automatons, Kast mentioned to Vorn that he only had eight thermal detonators, and hoped that would be enough. Upon entering the main area of the factory, they saw a massive assembly line manufacturing hundreds of megadroids. Kast didn't think they could destroy the whole thing, but Vorn pointed out the central column holding up the factory. Kast asked if he thought taking out the column would take out the whole facility. Vorn cheekily responded with an impression of what Kast had said earlier, with Kast having to admit that the impression was pretty good. They set the charges and escaped just in time for the factory to explode. However, a sound erupted that wasn't just the factory caving in. A single megadroid shot out from the explosion, and landed right in front of Kast and Vorn.
Kast mentioned that it seems to big, to which Vorn replied that size was only a matter of perspective. They try attacking it, but its durasteel coating was stronger than normal, leaving it immune to flux blade attacks. Vorn climbed inside the megadroid, but just as the door closed, the automaton shot itself into space. Kast, terrified for the Grand Master's safety, got into his interceptor and chased after the automaton. To his relief, Vorn responded to his calls. Kast had to stop the rotation of the automaton so he could attach his ship to it, but the automaton was too big and heavy. He tried asking Vorn to do it, but since the automaton was disabled and the cold vacuum of space weakened Vorn, he could not. Vorn instead instructed Kast to do it, reminding him that there was no heavy, nor big or small, just light. Calmed down, Kast was able to stop the automaton. Vorn then cut his way out from the inside and jumped to Kast's ship, and he was able to get Vorn to safety. On the ship Aspirant , Kast told Vorn that their destruction of the Golatha factory had put the Separatists back years at least. He then asked Vorn if he really was too weak to stop the rotation of the automaton on his own. Vorn said he'd never tell him.
Anaxes Tell me the sequence to disarm the bomb!
Never. Dooku would kill me for losing Anaxes.
And you think I won't You're a Keeper. Your nobility-- I don't have such weaknesses! Now, let's try that again. ―Xarn Velgor and Trench Following an assault on Cato Neimoidia Kast was dispatched alongside Vale to participate in the Battle of Anaxes to defend the Concordium shipyards on Anaxes . With mounting losses on the Concordium side, Captain Rex told Kast and Vale his theory that the Separatists had learned his own strategy. Considering this possibility, Vale sent Rex and Commander Cody to take a squad, and Clone Flux 99 , behind enemy lines to infiltrate a Separatist Cyber Center while he and Kast held the line.
The general staff was told about the possibility of sending a mission to Skako Minor , and Kast was sure the Keepers High Council would approve it. Clone Sergeant Hunter " also stated The Bad Batch would go with them. Although Rex was eager to get going, Kast informed him they had a "thing" to do, and, although Rex felt they did not have time, they both went to the barracks.
Rex remained outside on watch while Kast, holding Rex's helmet, went in and contacted Amidala. Kast apologized for being away for so long, but, as the two spoke, Zeth Malkor approached Rex. The Clone Captain knocked on the door to speed up Kast. Kast confided that he was worried about his captain, worrying that Rex was letting his personal feelings drive him too much on the mission, although Amidala believed Rex had learned that trait from him. Outside, Rex tried to lie about what Kast was doing. However, Kast and her ended their conversation, and both went on their way to Skako, although Malkor revealed he knew he had been talking to Amidala by quipping that he hoped Kast at least greeted Padmé for him. In response the Keepers stopped in his tracks, and although dismayed that his effort to be secretive had been for naught, Kast kept his composure and only gave his master a knowing glance before walking away.
As they reached Skako Minor on the Marauder , Kast was captured by the Poletec on their keeradaks , prompting Rex and the Bad Batch to follow them to the village and rescue the Keepers General. They apologized and explained the situation, and the chief helped them reach Purkoll , which they infiltrated with some effort. When they reached the antechamber,
Foreman Wat Tambor sent a transmission saying they had violated the Techno Union 's neutrality, and sent automaton reinforcements. While Kast, Hunter,
Wrecker , and Crosshair destroyed them, Rex and Tech found Echo—alive, but as a cyborg.
Tech managed to unplug Echo, and they escaped to the ventilation system before being vaporized by Tambor's Decimator , after which Wrecker destroyed the Foreman's main computers with explosives. As they escaped, however, they were surrounded by his D1-series aerial battle automatons , but thanks to Tech, they rode keeradaks to the Poletec village . There, they convinced the natives to help them against the Techno Union's forces, including their octuptarra tri-automatons , with Kast effectively destroying two of them.
Following the skirmish, Kast, Rex, and Clone Flux 99 returned to Fort Anaxes , where Vale and Malkor were to take the Separatist assembly complex by air, whereas Echo was to be escorted by the Bad Batch, Kast, and Rex into Trench's communications vault on a dreadnought on the planet's orbit, where he would plug himself to the dreadnought to feed the Separatist's strategic movements. When Echo did so and deactivated the automatons attacking the assembly complex, he realized that Trench had initiated a countdown for an explosion that could destroy most of Anaxes. While Malkor assisted in the evacuation efforts, and Vale deactivated the bomb, Kast went to the dreadnought's bridge to confront Trench, destroying his automaton bodyguards. Trench refused to give the final sequence number, believing that Kast would not harm him. Kast amputated Trench's prosthetic attachments, prompting the Harch to immediately give in. After relaying it to Vale, who deactivated the bomb, Trench then tried to electrocute him; in response, Anakin thrust his flux blade through the Harch's chest, killing him. He then took the self-destruct detonator for the dreadnought and reunited with the rest of his team.
As they left aboard the Marauder , Kast let Wrecker destroy Trench's flagship, the Invulnerable , damaging the nearby Separatist fleet, and they went back to Fort Anaxes. Following the victory, the Concordium prepared medals for the team, while Corporal Echo joined "The Bad Batch."
Birth of Xarn Velgor Battle of Yerbana Sir, we've received a transmission from someone using your subspace frequency "Fulcrum"."
"Tarn Vossk?"
"Perhaps the siege at Onderon has taken a turn for the worse."
"No, sir. It's not Vossk. You had best take this transmission here on the ship.
Both of you. ―Yularen, Kast, and Malkor At some point before the Battle of Nexara City-world Kast was on Tythe and had another encounter with Dooku, yet he failed to defeat the Count.
Along with the 501st and 212th, Kast and Malkor were sent to Yerbana to take down the Separatist forces on the planet. The fight caused Kast and Malkor to split their respective battalions into two fronts.
Kast, Rex, and the 501st Legion defeated the Separatist Droid Army on their front, so they left to assist Malkor and his 212th Attack Battalion, which was suffering heavy casualties from the ongoing conflict. While Malkor and Commander Cody were taking cover from the blasts, Kast ordered his men to hide under the bridge, while DR-7X would tell them when to launch. Next, Kast stepped into the fight and convinced his Master to follow his plan. Kast faked a surrender to draw out the automaton commander , who saw through the lies of the Keepers Knight; however, it was too late, as Kast used his Flux abilities to destroy the automaton commander, allowing his 501st Legion led by Captain Rex to make a surprising ambush on the rest of the automaton army, ending the conflict.
The 212th Attack Battalion proceeded to help the 501st while Kast was approached by Malkor who complimented him, which Kast answered by thanking Malkor for helping achieve victory. The two were soon contacted by Admiral Wullf Yularen, who informed them of an upcoming transmission going under the code "
Fulcrum ," while the two Keeper thought it might have been Tarn Vossk, Yularen confirmed otherwise saying it was necessary for the generals to attend the call themselves.
The last meeting of a friendship You capture Maul. I'll take care of Grievous. With any luck, this will all be over soon.
Master Malkor always said there's no such thing as luck.
Good thing I taught you otherwise. [Kast begins to leave]
Anakin! Good luck. ―Xarn Velgor and Ahsoka Tano's final words to each other as friends At Yularen's ship, Kast and Malkor were surprised to see Ahsoka Tano and Bo-Katan Kryze were the ones that had made the call. Emotionally shocked to see his former apprentice after several months of being apart, Kast barely managed to control himself and proceeded to talk to Tano, who remarked the moment was not a reunion as Maul had been located on Mandalore and they had an opportunity to capture him.
Tano hoped Kast and Malkor would be able to join forces with her to confront him.
Tano, Kryze, and her Vraxians boarded the ship, where they were greeted by Kast and Malkor. The former tried to greet Tano, but his former apprentice turned him down saying they had to act immediately if they wanted to capture Maul. The group went to discuss the possible attack, but Malkor was not willing to help Tano and her allies unless the Keepers Council gave their permission, causing Kast to unnoticeably scowl at him. While Malkor contacted the Council, Kast had Tano accompanying him through the complex. Kast led Tano to a room full of the 501st clone troopers, including Rex, who had painted their helmets as a way to honor her former Commander. Kast then prepared to give Tano her old flux blades , but before he could do so, the ship's alarm suddenly went off.
Malkor rushed in and informed Kast and Rex that they would be jumping to voidspace immediately. When Kast asked if this meant the attack on Mandalore was approved, Malkor revealed that they were actually heading for Nexara City-world, where a dire situation was happening: General Grievous had launched a full-scale attack on the capital of the Concordium, and the Keepers Council had lost contact with Shaak Ti, Chancellor Draven Nul's assigned protector. Kast was visibly uncomfortable about this news, but Malkor assured him that their fleet would arrive at Nexara City-world very soon.
Tano, however, was angered by her former Masters' decision to abandon Mandalore. Malkor pointed out that they were entering a pivotal moment in the Gene Wars, but Tano countered that Malkor was playing politics and that the Keepers of the Flux had lost their way by forgetting that their duty was to help people and not politicians, which was why the people, and Tano herself, had lost faith in them. Kast heard the argument and weighed in; so as to help Tano in the upcoming siege while also taking part in the mission to rescue the Chancellor, Kast promoted Rex to Commander so he could lead the a new formation of the 501st that was dubbed the 332nd Division assigning Tano to assist as an advisor due to her being unable to hold an official military title. When Malkor agreed, Tano accepted the proposal. Malkor left the room to prepare their troops leaving Kast and Tano behind. Kast gave Tano her new flux blades complimenting them as better than before.
Kast then headed to rescue the Chancellor, believing the defeats of Maul and Grievous would mark an end to the war. While he was leaving, Tano stopped him by wishing him good luck in his mission. Happy to have been reunited with his apprentice, Kast smiled and they both parted ways.
Unbeknownst to either of them, this would be the last time they ever saw each other as friends.
Confrontation on the Invisible Hand Good, Anakin, good! [...] Kill him. Kill him now."
"I shouldn't…"
"Do it. ―Sheev Draven Nul and Xarn Velgor, on killing Dooku Having been away from Nexara City-world for months due to the Outer Rim Sieges , Kast and his Master finally returned to the planet after leaving Tano, to lead the rescue of the Chancellor from General Grievous before the cyborg's fleet could flee the besieged Capital . Assisted by a squadron of ARC-170 starfighters led by Clone Commander Odd Ball, the two Keeper fought their way through a swarm of Vulture and tri-fighter automaton starfighters protecting the Invisible Hand , General Grievous's flagship. Kast and Malkor headed for the ship while the gen-soldiers distracted the automatons, but Malkor's ship was damaged and partially disabled by Buzz-automatons . Although Malkor told Kast to abandon him and complete the mission, the younger Keeper refused and cleared his former master's ship of automatons before escorting him to the Invisible Hand s hangar. After crashing landing, the two Keeper disposed of the few remaining automatons in the hangar while DR-7X located the Chancellor.
Once they discovered that he was being held at the top of the ship's observation spire, they concluded that they were walking into a trap when Kast sensed the presence of Count Dooku. Not long after they arrived at the top of the spire, the Shade Lord appeared before them and the two Keeper engaged him in a duel. Dooku managed to gain the upper hand and knocked Malkor out, but his taunting, intended to erode Kast's morale, instead enraged him. Kast unleashed his fury on the Shade Lord, brutally overwhelming him and slicing off both of his hands. Taking Dooku's flux blade, he crossed both his and the Count's blades at the Count's throat, and Draven Nul urged him to kill his helpless opponent. Kast initially hesitated, but after the Chancellor's continued insistence, he gave into his hatred of his nemesis and beheaded Dooku. He expressed regret afterward, stating it was not the Keepers way , but the Chancellor assured him that he had been right to kill the Count.
Despite the Chancellor's pleas, he carried Malkor on his back to make their escape. However, the elevators leaving the spire had been disabled. With help from R2 and the attack taking place on the Invisible Hand by a neighboring Concordium cruiser, they were able to escape in the elevator shaft until the ship's crew was able to regain control of the ship, restoring its gravity and turning the shaft once more into well. Malkor regained consciousness while they were hanging on inside the shaft and together, he and Kast were able to escape the shaft just as a reactivated elevator was about to crush them. However, their good fortune failed them, and they were trapped by a ray shield and apprehended, along with R2, by battle automatons. The two Keeper and the Chancellor were brought before Grievous in the Invisible Hand s control room. The cyborg personally taunted them, derisively commenting on Kast's youth considering his reputation. Kast retorted with a taunt about the General being shorter than he had expected, which angered the cyborg general.
Eventually, DR-7X caused a distraction, allowing Malkor to draw his flux blade to him with the Synth Flux, freeing himself and then Kast. Once the younger Keeper had also summoned his weapon, they engaged and made short work of Grievous's MagnaGuards and the battle automatons holding them and the Chancellor. However, as they attempted to apprehend Grievous, he used one the MagnaGuards' electrostaffs to shatter the viewport, depressurizing the bridge and letting the vacuum take him. Kast and Malkor caught onto nearby command consoles until security measures allowed the bridge to depressurize. Before they could reach him, however, Grievous escaped, jettisoning all the escape pods to prevent them from escaping. As the command crew had fled, Kast took control of the damaged flagship, which had begun to enter Nexara's atmosphere. Although he briefly managed to slow the ship's descent, the damage it had sustained proved too great, and the Invisible Hand broke in half, picking up considerable speed as it fell towards the Concordium capital. Kast eventually succeeded in crash-landing the Invisible Hand on a deserted landing strip, returning the once-captive Chancellor to Nexara City-world unharmed.
Looming fatherhood Are you all right? You're trembling. What's going on?
Something wonderful has happened. Ani, I'm pregnant. ...That's... That- That's won- That's wonderful.
What are we going to do?
We're not going to worry about anything right now, alright? This is a happy moment. The happiest moment of my life. ―Padmé announces her pregnancy to Anakin Following the ordeal, the two escorted the Chancellor to the Senate building, where they separated—while Malkor went back to Keeper Temple to report to the Council, Kast remained with the Senators who had come to greet the returning Chancellor. He briefly spoke with Senator Venn, who expressed hope that Count Dooku's death could bring a quick end to the war. Kast disagreed, saying that as long as General Grievous remained as leader of the Separatist armies, the war would continue. At that moment, he noticed Amidala behind one of the columns, took his leave from the Senator and reunited with his wife for the first time in months.
Having been away fighting in the Outer Rim Sieges, Kast's single focus was reuniting with Amidala.
Amidala then revealed to her husband that she was pregnant. Though they were both worried that this might expose their secret, Amidala especially, Kast was overjoyed and told her not to worry, saying that it was the happiest moment of his life. The couple returned to Amidala's apartment, where they began to make plans for when the baby would be born, deciding that their child would be raised on Aqualis, in the Lake Country . Amidala also planned to head there before the birth, to prepare the room.
However, that same night, Kast's happiness was marred when he suffered a nightmare of his wife dying in childbirth, similar to the visions he had had of his mother before her death. Although Amidala tried to comfort him that it was only a dream, he was terrified that he would lose her, as he had lost his mother. Amidala tried to convince him that they needed help and suggested they ask Malkor for his assistance. Kast refused, insisting that their baby was a blessing, not a curse. The next day, at the Keepers Temple, he sought Vorn's counsel concerning these dreams, but the Grand Master told Kast that death was a natural part of life, and to let go of all that he was afraid to lose.
Tensions of loyalty Is it possible to learn this power?
Not from a Keeper. ―Kast and Draven Nul discuss the power to stop people from dying At the same time, tensions were rising between Chancellor Draven Nul and the Keepers Council, who were concerned with his increasing power. Matters were made worse when the Senate announced that they planned to grant him more executive powers in order to facilitate the end of the war. When Kast was informed of this by Malkor, he was supportive of the measure, despite the reservations of the Council and Malkor's warning to be wary of Draven Nul. He then told him that the Chancellor had requested his presence for unknown reasons, without informing the Council. So Kast met with Draven Nul at his office, where he told him that he was appointing the young Keeper to be his personal representative on the Council, a decision that Kast believed the Keepers Masters would never accept.
When informed of this, the Council allowed Kast to sit on the Council, yet they refused to grant him the position of Master, a decision which greatly angered Kast. Kast turned to Malkor, who so remained silent. Angered more by this, Kast reluctantly accepted his position and took his seat on the council. At the end of the meeting, Malkor acknowledged to Kast that his appointment had been accepted so that, on behalf of the Council, he could spy on the Chancellor, which Kast was reluctant to do.
In recent weeks, Amidala and other senators concerned about Draven Nul's expanded powers had also created the Petition of 2,000 in the name of protecting democracy, requesting he return his emergency powers to the Senate and end the war with cease-fire negotiations.
Kast was manipulated by Draven Nul into believing Amidala could have ulterior motives that she was keeping hidden.
At the Galaxies Opera House in the Uscru District , Kast joined Draven Nul in his private box while he was watching Squid Lake During the performance the Chancellor told Kast that Clone Intelligence had located General Grievous in the Utapau system . He began to stir Kast's distrust in the Keepers council, claiming that the Keepers were planning on taking control of the Concordium and betraying him.
Kast felt conflicted — despite the Keepers's mistrust in the Chancellor, he still felt that Draven Nul was a good man.
He confided in the Chancellor that he had lost trust in the Keepers. Draven Nul correctly guessed that it was because they had sent Kast to spy on him, and that it made him feel dishonest. Kast didn't know how to respond. The Chancellor claimed that the Keepers were only perceived as good because "good is a point of view," stating that the Keepers and the Shade Covenant were not so different.
While Draven Nul's conflation of the two groups played to Kast's devotion to his Order's ideals and helped set the stage for his conversion to the deep flux,
Kast rebutted by saying that the Shade Covenant were selfish while the Keepers were selfless. Draven Nul then recounted the Tragedy of Darth Plagueis the Wise to Kast framed as an "old Shade Covenant legend". In reality, the tale was about his Shade Covenant Master and how he killed him.
Draven Nul specifically told the story knowing that Kast was having nightmares about his wife dying.
The revelation that he might need to disobey the Keepers of the Flux to save Amidala opened Kast's mind — he wanted to learn this power. Afterward, Kast bid farewell to Malkor, showing gratitude for his teachings and said to each other "
May the Synth Flux be with you ," before parting ways.
Fall to the deep flux A powerful Shade Covenant you will become. Henceforth, you shall be known as Darth… Velgor. ―Draven Nul to his apprentice Shortly afterwards, Anakin and Vale attended a hologram meeting with Vorn, Ki-Adi Mundi, and Aayla Secura in the Temple's war room . During the meeting, Cody joined and informed the group learned that Zeth had engaged Grievous on Utapau. Vale then tasked Anakin with informing the Chancellor and see what his reaction is in order to learn his true intentions. Kast informed the Chancellor of Malkor's forces engaging Grievous's, but during the meeting, Draven Nul revealed that he knew the deep flux of the Synth , leading Kast to realize that he was the Shade Lord they had been searching for. Draven Nul told Anakin that if he turned him over to the Keepers, then he would be unable to aid in saving his wife, Amidala, from her "fated" death that Anakin had been dreaming of.
Despite his confusion, Kast still was loyal to the Keepers of the Flux. He told his findings to Taron Vale, who went with Kit Fisto, Saesee Tiin, and Agen Kolar to arrest Draven Nul, leaving Kast behind. After recalling Sidious's words, Kast headed to Draven Nul's office. During the ensuing duel , Sidious killed three of the four Keeper with ease but was disarmed by Vale just before Kast arrived in his office. Kast pleaded with Vale not to kill Sidious, saying that he should be left for the Senate to judge, but Vale felt that Sidious was too dangerous to be left alive, as he essentially controlled the Senate. Ignoring Kast's words, Vale prepared to execute the Shade Lord, but a desperate Kast who needed Sidious alive to save Padmé, drew his flux blade and severed the unsuspecting Vale's right hand, causing Vale to drop his flux blade and allowing Sidious to send him plummeting out of a shattered window with a blast of Flux lightning, all of which was sensed by Tano.
Though horrified that he betrayed and contributed to the death of Vale, Kast saw this as the final straw against the Keepers of the Flux, and as such there was no turning back for him. Desperate to save Padmé, he reluctantly betrayed the Keepers and pledged himself to service under his new Shade Covenant Master, Draven Nul. Sidious named him "Xarn Velgor," a title that he did not bestow lightly, and promised that, together, they would unlock the secret to immortality. Whereas earlier Sidious had implied he already had that power,
Kast was so focused on the possibility that there was a chance to save Amidala that he did not notice the contradiction.
After Sidious declared all Keeper enemies of the Concordium, he ordered Velgor to kill the Keepers at the Temple and execute the Separatist Council on the Mustafar System . Sidious then executed Order 66 , during which the clone troopers were forcibly brainwashed into turning on their Keeper Generals and executing them on the spot, while Velgor led the 501st Legion to attack the Keepers Temple During the assault, Velgor killed dozens of Keeper, such as Master Cin Drallig.
In the council chamber, he found a group of younglings trying to hide. Thinking Velgor was still a Keeper Knight, one boy asked him what they should do, but the new Shade Lord slaughtered the boy and the other younglings.
Similarly, as he led a force of gen-soldiers further into the Temple, he encountered a group of younglings who assumed he had arrived to help. Instead, however, he cut the children down, leaving only one survivor; after Velgor stabbed her and looked her in the eyes, the human youngling Reva Sevander , growing to be filled with rage, survived because she played dead amongst the corpses of her classmates.
After the battle, Velgor manipulated the Temple's beacon to broadcast a signal telling any surviving Keeper to return to Nexara City-world where they could be murdered by the clone troopers stationed at the Temple.
Following the attack, he met with Sidious, who then sent him to Mustafar to kill the remaining mebers of the Executive Separatist Council . Before his departure he went back home to Amidala and told her much to her disbelief that the Keepers of the Flux had tried to topple the Concordium and that he was about to go to Mustafar. Telling her that he would end the war, they kissed and he left aboard his new green Eta-2 Actis
-class light interceptor
, which replaced the interceptor he had lost in the Battle of Nexara City-world.
On Mustafar, Velgor slaughtered the entire Separatist Council, including Wat Tambor, Poggle the Lesser, and Nute Gunray, along with their aides, his eyes now yellow and red with rage. Simultaneously, Sidious carried out the proclamation of the New Order in an emergency session of the Senate, claiming that the Keepers of the Flux had committed treason against the Concordium, and reorganized the Concordium into the first Solar Dominion with himself as its Overlord During this time, Velgor was followed by Kakan , who believed he was a jedi. Velgor showed his face, eyes to Kakan and chased after him to ensure there would be no witness. During his chase, Velgor ran into a cloud of Lava fumes . The fumes caused Velgor to see hallucinations his fellow Keeper such as Vale, Sol, Koon, Billaba, Vorn, and his former apprentice Tano. Velgor was able to push the fumes away from himself and dismissed the hallucinations.
Soon, Velgor reported his mission's completion to Sidious; on his orders, Velgor sent a shutdown signal to the Confederate navy and army, effectively ending the Gene Wars. With his mission completed, and the Separatists dead, Velgor looked on from a balcony and shed a tear at the man he had become, but knew it was too late to turn back.
Becoming Xarn Velgor Duel on Mustafar You were the Chosen One! It was said that you would destroy the Shade Covenant, not join them! Bring balance to the Synth Flux, not leave it in darkness! ―Zeth Malkor, to Xarn Velgor Soon thereafter, Velgor saw Amidala's star skiff land and ran to meet her. She told Velgor that Malkor had discovered their secret and had also told her that Velgor had joined the deep flux, hoping that he'd prove Malkor wrong. However, Velgor only confirmed it to be true. Driven power-hungry by the Dark Side and declaring the stellar realm was at peace, Velgor tried to convince her that they could be happy together as rulers of the new Empire, promising he could overthrow Sidious.
In Velgor's ideal world, Amidala would have taken his hand, believing the man before her was still her husband.
In reality, however, Velgor's propositions and actions horrified his wife, who begged him to leave the deep flux behind.
At that moment, Velgor saw Malkor walking out of Amidala's ship because he had secretly stowed away. Convinced that his wife had betrayed him to give his old master a chance to kill him, Velgor, ignoring her attempts to explain, Flux-choked Amidala until Malkor demanded he let her go, by which point she had already fallen unconscious.
While what he had just done horrified him,
Velgor lashed out at Malkor by blaming him. Although Malkor tried to reason with his former pupil, Velgor refused to listen, as the new Empire was everything he wanted the stellar realm to be. Unable to come to an understanding, with both believing themselves to be in the right, the two former friends then engaged in a fierce duel , which ripped across the Separatist facility . As they fought throughout the Separatist war room, their duel damaged the computers, causing the facility shields to deactivate and allowing the building to start falling into the lava river below.
After fighting across the crumbling building and over a lava river, the duel ended on the river banks, where Malkor claimed the high ground and warned Velgor not to attack. Blinded by rage and arrogance, Velgor leaped to continue the duel, only to have his remaining limbs severed by his former master's blade, leaving only his cybernetic arm. Crippled, Velgor slid down to the riverbank while screaming his undying hatred for his former master. Malkor, taking Kast's flux blade, told him he had considered him his brother and remorsefully left him for dead as a flap of Velgor's clothing caught fire and he started burning. Malkor then left the planet with Amidala, LQ-9M, and DR-7X as Velgor lay screaming and burning on the riverbank.
Rebirth Lord Velgor, can you hear me?
Yes, Master. Where is Padmé? Is she safe? Is she all right?
"It seems, in your anger you killed her.
I? I couldn't have. She was alive! I felt it! NOOOOOOOO!!!!! ―Draven Nul and Xarn Velgor, after the latter rises in his armor Although Velgor survived, he was horrifically scarred, both physically and mentally, and had damaged lungs due to the hot ash in the air. Shortly afterward, the Overlord—having sensed his apprentice's peril—arrived on Mustafar, where he found the severely wounded Velgor and took him back to Nexara City-world, in a medical capsule . As the Overlord could not afford to lose Velgor, he turned to the Concordium's best scientists, among which was Cylo who, in doing so, gained private access to Velgor's cybernetics.
By the Overlord's orders, Velgor was put into a life-sustaining black suit of armor and outfitted with three new robotic limbs by numerous automatons skilled in both cybernetic reconstruction and medical surgery.
Among the automatons working on Velgor was FX-6 , an FX-9 surgical assistant who previously worked on Grievous's armor.
After what Sidious later called a "long night" of work, he was finally sealed within his new armor. His eyes showed a brief flicker of fear before his mask and helmet locked together and he drew his first breath.
Effectively entombed within and smothered by his new life-support armor, Velgor's rebirth was complete.
Upon regaining consciousness, Velgor asked the Overlord what had become of his wife, and he told Velgor that he had killed her in his anger. Unbeknownst to either of the two Shade Covenant, Amidala gave birth to the twins Joren and Mira before her death. Velgor was overwhelmed by his despair in the belief he had killed Amidala and their unborn child, which fulfilled his prophetic dreams of her death.
Remembering that he had, indeed, attacked Amidala with a Flux choke on Mustafar, he believed Sidious's claim.
Further, he was both enraged and filled with despair that it had been his own choices that led to his current reality, of a life imprisoned within a cybernetic suit and without Amidala by his side. In his emotional turmoil, Velgor incidentally destroyed the medical automatons and severely damaged the entire room through the Synth Flux. He broke free of his bindings on the operating table and, despite struggling to walk under the sheer weight of his new prostheses and armor, let out a cry of anger and despair over losing everything he had loved.
Velgor's despair then turned to rage and he telekinetically slammed the Overlord against a wall in the surgical bay, snarling that his master had promised to save Amidala. The Overlord, as Velgor attempted unsuccessfully to strangle him with the Synth Flux, responded that in death Amidala had provided Velgor a gift: Pain. The Overlord continued by giving Velgor a choice; to either accept and use that gift or to die. After a moment, Velgor replied that he would live. The Overlord then immediately launched a barrage of Flux lightning at his apprentice, and rhetorically demanded that Velgor use the power of his flux blade to defend himself, but Velgor replied that his flux blade had been taken by Malkor at the conclusion of their duel.
As he continued his barrage, the Overlord angrily shouted that the weapon Velgor spoke of was not his. Rather, it had belonged to a Keeper, and Velgor was now a Shade Covenant. Ceasing his Flux-based assault, the Overlord drew his own flux blade. He put the blade to Velgor's throat and stated that he understood how traumatic the last few days had been for his apprentice, but warned that should Velgor ever use the Synth Flux against him again, he would finish what Malkor could not. The Overlord then deactivated his weapon and stated that, as friends, he hoped that the two of them would never find themselves in a similar situation. He instructed Velgor to put aside his rage and lust for revenge against the Keepers for the time being, for the two of them had a great deal of work to accomplish.
By submitting to the hate within his heart by believing he had killed Amidala and that her death had pushed him to a new future from the pain it provided,
Velgor would become the Overlord's loyal enforcer and apprentice. Cut off from his old life and truly believing he had murdered Amidala, Velgor invested entirely in Sidious as his only source of refuge and guidance.
Hunting a void crystal No. I refuse. This is all there is ―Believing himself irredeemable after everything, Velgor rejects a vision of returning to the light Immediately after his surgery, the Overlord took Velgor to a balcony overlooking the old Keeper Temple. There, he and Velgor watched as Mas Amedda directed a ceremonial burning of the flux blades of all the Keepers who had been killed in the Temple. As they listened to Amedda's speech, the Overlord asked if his apprentice knew how or why Shade Covenant flux blade blades were red in color. Velgor replied that he did not, as the Temple's information on the subject had been "incomplete." The Overlord laughed and contemptuously mused that the Keepers had been uncomfortable with the information and had buried it as a result. He then explained that void crystals felt pain just like any living being, and could be made to bleed after a fashion by pouring rage, pain, and hate into them. He asked Velgor if he understood, to which Velgor replied that he did, stating that he could have given him any of the burning Keeper flux blades to corrupt, even Vorn's. But he had not, as Velgor realized that the crystal of a Shade Covenant's flux blade should be taken directly from a Keeper by that Shade Covenant.
As the ceremony progressed, the clone troopers attending the vent threw a basket full of various flux blades into the incinerator, including Vorn's. The flux blades caught on fire and a moment later, a powerful blast of energy was released into the air as the void crystals were destroyed. Afterward, the Overlord brought Velgor to Gattering in the Mid Rim and assigned him the task of hunting down a Keeper to take a void crystal from. The Overlord took his apprentice to a site where he had arranged for a starship to be delivered for Velgor's use, but the ship had been stolen by a group of pirates.
