Velgor demanded the answers to his questions about his master, to which the Eye granted him a Shade Covenant wayfinder . After leaving the cave, Velgor came upon his old Keeper interceptor, where he was then met by Ochi and his Droid Crush Pirates While fighting off the pirates, Velgor cornered Ochi and knocked him out before reclaiming his flux blade. He then finished off the pirates and used their parts to repair the damaged interceptor. Once the ship was repaired, Velgor used the now captured Ochi to access the wayfinder, which showed the way to a planet called Exegol . With Ochi trapped in a escape pod beneath the ship, Velgor flew the interceptor away from Mustafar and used a voidspace transport ring to journey toward his next destination.
Finding Exegol Unlimited power. Beyond anything you have ever experienced. All in my hands. You feel it burning your flesh and mind. Ready to incinerate you in an instant. And you know you cannot fight it. So how… how can you claim it? ―Draven Nul, to Xarn Velgor After exiting voidspace, Velgor and Ochi found themselves in the Red Honeycomb Zone as the Shade Covenant attempted to locate Exegol. However, they were then met by a massive, tentacled space-faring creature related to the Summa-verminoth Before Velgor could react, he was attacked by a fleet of Dominion Cruisers led by Sly Moore and Admiral Corleque . Velgor fought against the fleet's Razor drones until he had an idea. Changing his course, he flew toward the massive creature and allowed the Razor drones to follow until they were crushed by the creature's tentacles. As Velgor flew past the creature, Moore warned him that it would break his mind. Ignoring the Umbaran's warning, he continued on until he and Ochi were both hit by mental attacks. Velgor experienced several traumatic visions from his past, which concluded with a vision of his son killing him in battle and taking his place by the Overlord's side.
Upon escaping the Red Nebula, Velgor finally reached the planet Exegol, where he and Ochi promptly crashed. Breaking free from the wrecked interceptor, they were both met once again by the creature. But this time, however, Velgor did not run away. Disobeying his master's command, he reached out with the deep flux and used it to bring the creature down, thus allowing him and Ochi to continue on towards the Overlord's Shade Covenant Citadel Standing atop the creature, Velgor commanded his master to show himself. And once he did, Velgor rode the creature into battle. In turn, the Overlord summoned two crustaceous monsters to attack the creature, though they were both quickly crushed. When Velgor told his master that he was no longer his apprentice, the Overlord merely scoffed and reached out with the Synth Flux himself, twisting and contorting the creature until it broke apart. Forced to return to ground level, Velgor followed the Overlord into the Citadel, where his greatest secrets lie in wait.
Once inside, Velgor was greeted by a menagerie of horrors forged by his master. All around him were large vats containing bioengineered organisms strong in the deep flux of the Synth. Along with these organisms were large Shade Covenant statues, dozens of Tanker guards Shade Covenant cultists , and a jar containing a severed hand. Velgor was quickly attacked by the guards and cultists, but he managed to defeat them all before venturing further into the Citadel. Ochi caught up with Velgor and attempted to dissuade him, but the Shade Covenant carried on until he reached a level beneath the Citadel. This level was lined with hundreds of Dominion Cruisers, each armed with a planet-destroying cannon . Showing no fear, Velgor continued on into a massive chamber, which held a dome filled with red light and piercing screams. As Ochi tried to dissuade him once more, the Shade Covenant entered the dome and stepped into the burning light.
Within the dome was a subterranean mountain of Kyber crystal, bled red by the Shade Covenant cultists who kept it in constant pain. The Overlord explained that he did this in order to power the cannons of his fleet. When the crystal was cut, it screamed in pain again and sent out a wave of power, knocking off Ochi's helmet and burning his unprotected eyes. Velgor suffered as well while his master mocked him for attempting to claim his power. The Overlord told his apprentice that if he chose to walk with him, he would never escape his pain. But only by walking with him would he be able to share his power. The Overlord then asked his apprentice if he had chosen, to which Xarn Velgor replied that Sidious was his master. Even though he experienced a vision, in which Joren Kast told him that he would, one day, kill the Overlord,
Velgor had seen the power Sidious commanded on Exegol, so he fully decided to return to his master's service, departing Exegol with Sidious, Ochi, Mas Amedda, and Sly Moore.
Hunting Joren Kast A new target You could still be strong, my son. I know there is power in you. But I have seen the true might and purpose of the Shade Covenant. And there is no room for another. So this time…you will die. ―Xarn Velgor Upon returning to Nexara City-world, Velgor was brought to the very building where he had been transformed to undergo repairs. A medical automaton told him to prepare for shutdown, but Velgor refused, choosing instead to remain conscious throughout the procedure. Once he was fully repaired, Velgor asked the Overlord his will, to which his master told him to decide that for himself. Velgor then called over Ochi, who had just been given a cybernetic visor so he could continue to see. With Ochi at his side, Velgor left Nexara City-world in a Lambda-class shuttle and decided that he would hunt down and kill his son, believing that there was no place for him as an apprentice after the power he had seen on Exegol.
Truthfully, however, Velgor still wished Kast had joined him on Cloud City.
Traveling to a remote outpost somewhere in the Outer Rim, Velgor and Ochi met with a Hutt named Bokku . When Ochi asked for the whereabouts of Dax Corbin, Bokku reported that he had been stolen and assured the assassin that Jabba would recover him. However, Ochi then told Bokku that he would recover Corbin himself in exchange for power. Bokku then asked Ochi if he served the Overlord, to which Ochi replied that they both served Velgor now.
Velgor was then attacked by a group of Gamorrean guards, which the Shade Lord slew with ease. Bokku quickly apologized for not recognizing Velgor and reiterated that he did not know where Corbin was. From this, Ochi determined that Jabba had likely hired someone to find Corbin and asked Bokku for their identity. Bokku expressed fear at the idea of betraying Jabba until Ochi pried into Bokku's willingness to take over the Grand Hutt Council . Bokku then revealed that he had been tracking the hunters who had taken Jabba's bounty and that one crew of automatons had gone in a different direction from the others. Bokku told Velgor the automatons were deep in the heart of Hutt Space and that it would be better to go in Bokku's barge so as not to scare them away. On their way to the automatons' location, Bokku inquired as to his compensation for his assistance, to which Velgor assured him that he would be rewarded. Under the piloting of Bokku's nav automaton Gak-Sixtoo , they arrived at Zee-Nine City Seven while Velgor and Ochi boarded their Lambda shuttle. But as they approached the city, their ship's engines were shut down, causing them to crash.
After escaping the wreckage, Velgor and Ochi were confronted by the Droid Crush Pirates, who expressed shock at Ochi's service to Velgor. When Ochi claimed he had picked the winning side, the automaton captain told him he had picked wrong as IG-88 revealed himself and fired upon both Ochi and Velgor. As Velgor deflected IG-88's attacks, the automaton reminded his target that he had sustained damages while IG-88 himself had not. Velgor informed the automaton that he had information he required and commanded him to lay down his weapons, to which IG-88 replied that Velgor would only access his information by downloading it from his severed head. Velgor went to attack IG-88, but the automaton then took out a remote and used it to slice into Velgor's armor, taking control of the Shade Lord. As IG-88 forced Velgor to hold his own saber to his head, Velgor demanded to know who had given the automaton the code to breach his armor.
IG-88 responded that the terms of his employment prevented him from sharing that information. While the automaton was distracted, Velgor used the Synth Flux to take the device from him, allowing him to use it against his attacker. Velgor then forced IG-88 to hold his own pulse rifle to his head and pull the trigger, incapacitating him. Velgor then continued his fight against the pirates until Bokku's barge fired upon him. Velgor blocked the blasts with his flux blade and redirected the energy at the pirates, knocking them back along with Ochi. As the pirates retreated, Velgor took IG-88's hand and used it to access a terminal he had been using earlier. Upon activating the terminal, Velgor and Ochi discovered various dead ends that the automaton had been chasing, as well as the symbol of crime syndicate Crimson Dawn . After acquiring IG-88's information, Velgor returned to Bokku's barge and confronted him over his betrayal. When Velgor asked the Hutt what he had learned, Bokku conceded that the Shade Lord could not be defeated.
Pursuing Crimson Dawn What you see is what you get with Ochi of Bestoon. I'll always obey the most powerful. So as long as that's really you Always. ―Ochi and Xarn Velgor Investigating Crimson Dawn's involvement, Velgor sent Ochi to Arkanis to gather information on the syndicate. As Ochi prepared to sneak into a bar, Velgor told him to walk in the front door instead. After a brief scuffle, Ochi retrieved an invitation sent out by the syndicate and showed it to Velgor. He and Velgor then returned to Bokku's lair to interrogate him over the invitation. The Hutt revealed that Crimson Dawn was holding an auction for Corbin and that Jabba would repurchase him. However, Velgor told Bokku that he would bid against Jabba and lose. This would humiliate him and make Jabba lose all fear of him, allowing Bokku to betray him. Once Bokku agreed, Velgor and Ochi returned to the Lambda-class shuttle and began to take off. But before they could leave the planet, Velgor grabbed Ochi and dropped him from the ship. Once Ochi hit the ground, Velgor ordered him to find out who else Bokku was working for. Later that night, Velgor observed as Ochi was offered a place in Crimson Dawn by a group of assassins. However, Ochi rejected their offer and managed to eliminate them.
Velgor then approached Ochi, who quickly realized that Velgor already knew that Bokku was working with Crimson Dawn. He had only wished to test the Ochi's loyalty. As more assassins surrounded them, Ochi reaffirmed he would always obey Velgor as long as he remained the most powerful. Velgor assured Ochi that he always would be as the two of them set about eliminating the assassins.
After discovering that Sly Moore had hired IG-88, Velgor and Ochi returned to Nexara City-world to find her, her court and the rebuilt automaton bounty hunter in Administrative Temple Garden 313A . As Velgor struck down IG-88 once more, Moore and her court fled into a nearby temple. As they attempted to escape into the streets, Velgor used the Synth Flux to close the door in front of them. However, Moore then took out another remote and used it to freeze Velgor in his own armor, allowing her and her court to open fire on the Shade Lord. But this did not last as Velgor overcame the Umbaran's control and struck down her entire court. Moore tried once more to take control of Velgor, but Ochi revealed that Velgor had fixed that vulnerability in his armor. Velgor then knocked the remote out of Moore's hand and told her that she could not bend his will.
Moore responded that she only wanted to see if Velgor was still plagued by fear, to which Velgor said that the only fear in that place was hers. Moore conceded that he could kill her effortlessly, but also pointed out that doing so would do nothing to save him from Joren Kast. When Velgor asked the Umbaran what she knew of Kast, she stated that the key to finding him, his friend Dax Corbin was currently being sold by Crimson Dawn. She also pointed out that if she secured Corbin for Velgor, then the Overlord would not learn of his plans. When Ochi asked Moore why Velgor should trust her, the Umbaran answered that she would be delighted to reunite Velgor with the only person who could kill him, to which Velgor agreed.
Auction for Dax Corbin Who trained you? Who was your master?
Someone who knew quite a bit about you, Lord Velgor. And your master as well. ―Xarn Velgor and Lady Qi'ra duel aboard the Vermillion Shortly after, Ochi and Sly Moore left with a few Dominion officers to attend the Auction for Dax Corbin . However, this was merely a ruse to humiliate the Umbaran in the Overlord's eyes. Amidst the bidding, Velgor himself arrived at the auction on Jekara along with a pair of death troopers. After cutting down a few Crimson Dawn guards, Velgor infiltrated the Vermillion just as Crimson Dawn's leader,
Lady Qi'ra sold Corbin to Jabba the Hutt.
Velgor then declared that Corbin belonged to him.
Velgor's presence sent Lyra Venn, who had infiltrated the auction on behalf of the Tagge Corporation with the smuggler Sana Starros , falling to the ground in a brief panic attack, but she managed to escape the room with Starros before the preoccupied Shade Lord could spot her. After his attention was briefly diverted to the sounds of Lyra Venn's panicked fall, he turned his attention back to his mission at hand.
As Velgor ordered the death troopers to transport Corbin to his flagship, Jabba pointed out that he had already laid claim to the smuggler. Moore attempted to explain the situation, but Velgor chastised her for her failure and warned her that the Overlord would hear of it. The Shade Lord then told Jabba that he and everyone else within the Dominion belonged to the Overlord, to which Jabba feigned amusement. Velgor went on to point out that the Dominion could easily replace the Hutts, while also pointing out the fear he could sense in Jabba and the rest of the attending Hutts. When Bokku angrily interrupted, Velgor asked Jabba who he was, to which Jabba said that he was no one. Jabba then finally conceded and allowed Corbin to be taken with his compliments and regards to the Overlord. As Velgor approached Corbin, Lady Qi'ra informed him that she would not get in his way, but also still required the one million credits she was promised. When Velgor told her she was owed nothing, Qi'ra inquired if the Dominion was too poor or cheap to pay its debts. Out of patience, Velgor drew out his flux blade, to which Qi'ra bared her own pair of blades.
As they dueled Velgor recognized that she was no threat to him, yet nonetheless found her level of skill, which included knowledge of the Teräs Käsi fighting form, notable, and recognized Qi'ra's fighting style as that of his master's.
When Velgor asked her who had trained her, Qi'ra only replied that she had been trained by someone who knew quite a bit about both Velgor and his master; in truth, she had been trained by Maul as part of his revenge plot against the Shade Covenant.
Velgor then pointed out that she did not have the Synth Flux and warned that her skill would not save her. After a brief struggle, Velgor pushed Qi'ra back with the Synth Flux, knocking her into Corbin. Stating she would pay the price for her foolishness, he raised his flux blade and prepared to strike her down. But he was suddenly distracted the arrival of Joren Kast, whose presence he sensed through the Synth Flux.
While Qi'ra retreated, Velgor was informed by General Romodi that Drax had established a secure channel to Kast's starfighter. Accessing the channel, Velgor told Kast he had Corbin in his possession. He then warned his son that if he did not come to him, then he would cut his friend in half.
Kast refused however, telling Velgor to come to him. Velgor conceded to this, ordering Romodi to bring Corbin aboard his Dominion Cruiser and to prepare his fighter.
Chase above Jekara I will leave you now, young Kast. My goal here is complete. I sense your terror at the idea of facing me again. Your skill in the Synth Flux does not matter. Only your fear . Because of it, you cannot threaten me, and you cannot threaten the Dominion.
You are no Keeper That's true, Velgor. But the next time we meet…I will be. ―Xarn Velgor and Joren Kast After boarding his TIE Advanced, Velgor took off in pursuit of his son. During the chase, Kast used his Starhawk to kick up a wave of water, which instantly froze and collided with Velgor's fighter. Though Velgor crashed, he was able to lift his ship out of the water with the Synth Flux and resume his pursuit.
After freeing his Starhawk from a group of pirates, Velgor fired at Kast, damaging one of his ship's stabilizers and causing him to crash back down onto the planet below. As Velgor closed in on Kast, he was contacted by Sly Moore. When Velgor stated that he did not require her assistance, Moore informed him that she was not helping him. Velgor was then attacked by the Droid Crush Pirates, who landed on his ship and began to tear it apart.
After causing some damage to the Shade Covenant's fighter, the pirates fell onto Kast's Starhawk and quickly repaired it for him, allowing him to take off from the planet. As Velgor continued his chase while fending off the pirates, he was informed by Ochi that the Hutts were going after the Dominion shuttle transporting Corbin.
Velgor was then contacted by Admiral Drax, who informed him of the Hutts' attack on the Imperator . However, Velgor chastised Drax for disturbing him, claiming that the activities of the Hutts were irrelevant to him. He then warned Drax that if Corbin was not aboard his flagship by the time he returned, he would require an explanation in person. Later in the fight, Drax contacted Velgor again and told him that he had appraised the Overlord of the situation. Since the Overlord considered the Dominion's arrangement with the Hutts to be valuable, he wished for it to be preserved by having Velgor eliminate the Grand Hutt Council. Velgor conceded to the Overlord's wishes and abandoned his pursuit of Kast, reminding his son that he was no Keeper, though the boy then claimed that the next time they met, he would be.
Velgor then boarded the Hutt Council's ship and summarily slaughtered them all, including Bokku. Before his death, Bokku revealed his allegiance to Crimson Dawn and had Gak-Sixtoo blow up their own ship in an attempt to kill Velgor, which the Shade Covenant survived by using the force to rip apart the ship and use the metal pieces to form a large protective cocoon in which he was protected from the blast and then fell back down to Jekara.
With the Hutt Council eliminated, the Hutts ceased their attack on the Imperator while Ryn reclaimed Corbin from the Dominion and delivered him to Jabba on Vorath Prime.
Shortly after, Velgor was informed by Lieutenant Jyala Haydenn that Beilert Valance had infiltrated the Imperator . He then found Valance trying to escape on a ship and used the Synth Flux to pull him from space, rendering him unconscious and taking him prisoner.
Killing the Dawn Hunting the new menace Crimson Dawn has barely shown its strength. To get this far, it must have agents at every level throughout the Dominion. Find these infiltrators, Lord Velgor…and show them who is stronger. ―Draven Nul, to Xarn Velgor With the Hutts dealt with, Velgor returned to the Imperator to confront Sly Moore, who had been captured by Ochi during the battle for trying to help in Kast's escape. When Moore revealed that the Overlord had wanted Kast alive, Velgor revealed that the boy had fled from him, declaring that he was not who the Overlord believed he could be. When Moore pointed out that Kast was still free and could still fight, the Shade Lord choked her and stated that his hope would not be enough. Velgor was then contacted by Draven Nul, prompting him to release the Umbaran and kneel before him. After restating his loyalty to his master, Velgor was instructed by Sidious to hunt down and eliminate Crimson Dawn's agents within the Dominion. Shortly after, Velgor was met on the Imperator by Romodi, who presented him with all of the troops he would need to destroy Crimson Dawn. Velgor then instructed Ochi to meet with the Assassin's Guild and inform them that they would be serving him now.
In secret, Ochi swore himself to Qi'ra's service after he arrived at the Assassin's Guild base, although the assassin would later claim he was acting as an Dominion double agent.
Following Ochi's departure, Velgor had Valance repaired and waited for him to awaken. Once he did, he was shocked to find himself restored to his former appearance. When Valance asked Velgor what he had done to him, Velgor explained that he had had Valance fixed since he would now be serving him.
Valance then realized that his heart was gone, to which the Shade Covenant informed him that it had been removed and replaced with cybernetics, along with further upgrades to his body. Valance attacked Velgor, but Velgor cut him in half before leaving him and telling a medical automaton to put him back together. Once Valance was repaired, Velgor, alongside Haydenn, informed him that he would be assassinating Crimson Dawn's agents within the Dominion. For if he did not, the Dominion would kill those he cared about.
While he meditated aboard the Imperator , Velgor was informed by Haydenn that Valance had completed his first assignment in killing the alleged Crimson Dawn mole Tarl Sokoli . Velgor then revealed that there was no connection between Sokoli and Crimson Dawn. Sokoli had been publicly dissatisfied with the Dominion and Velgor had decided that killing someone from Valance's childhood would be a good test for the bounty hunter. The Shade Covenant then told Haydenn to alert Ochi that Valance was ready to join them.
Velgor also came to realize that Ochi had joined with Crimson Dawn but, deciding he could use it to his advantage in the hunt for the syndicate, kept his deduction a secret.
Confronting the Knights of Ren You…and your rabble…you are…the Knights of Ren? Pathetic. ―Xarn Velgor, to Ren Velgor learned that Zahra's campaign against the Coalition ended in failure during the Battle of Panisia Sometime after that engagement,
Velgor investigated the assassination of the Overlord's Royal Guard on Nexara City-world, which was secretly carried out in part by Ochi.
During the investigation however, he was informed by Vaneé that a band of thieves had infiltrated Fortress Velgor. The Dark Lord quickly returned to Mustafar and confronted the intruders in his castle; a group of deep flux marauders known as the Knights of Ren , who were secretly working with Crimson Dawn. After Velgor killed one of them,
Fyodor with a Flux choke, their fought him with a flux blade while the rest of them retreated. The leader said he had taken the blade from his predecessor , from whom he also inherited his name; Ren.
Velgor expressed pity for how far the Knights of Ren had fallen before Ren leapt from a window with a device known as the Screaming Key . As Ren gloated below, Velgor used the Synth Flux to pull the key back up toward his hand, prompting the knights to open fire on him. Ren then pulled the key back down into his own hand before escaping with the knights on speeders. As the knights sped away, Velgor stepped outside to study the remains of his slaughtered troops.
Rooting out the Dawn Crimson Dawn is just a symptom . I will destroy the disease ―Xarn Velgor While thinking in his mediation chamber, Velgor reflected on his promise to his son on Cloud City that they would bring order to the stellar realm. Velgor concluded that his master had only brought about chaos and decided to set out on a new quest to establish order, whether or not he had his son by his side. Resolving to destroy Crimson Dawn as the first step, Velgor left with Ochi to assist the Dominion in hunting down Dawn agents on various planets, including Vincorba and Calior . While on Vincorba, they fought alongside a group of mercenaries known as the Revengers . With Velgor's crew established, Ochi commented that they would serve well as expendable assets, to which Velgor reminded him that he was expendable as well.
After finishing on Calior, Velgor, Ochi, and the Revengers rendezvoused with Valance and a few other assassins on Laecor to continue their hunt. When Valance asked Velgor why he was there instead of leading Dark Squadron , the Shade Lord simply told him to do as he was told. As a squad of legion vanguard attempted to move a group of civilians, the civilians refused to comply, prompting the legion vanguard to fire on them. However, Velgor then jumped in to deflect their pulse rifle bolts and slaughter them. When Ochi showed confusion over his master's actions, Velgor revealed that the legion vanguard served Crimson Dawn. As the group carried on, Velgor came upon a statue, which the locals said was of their village guardian who was killed fighting Crimson Dawn.
Upon locating more of the Dawn's agents, Ochi opted to use the Revengers as bait to lure them out of their stronghold. Velgor then used the Synth Flux to grab Ochi and throw him toward the Revengers, using him as bait as well. As the agents surrounded them, Ochi requested that Velgor bomb the stronghold. But Velgor refused and instead mounted Gee-Ninety , balancing himself atop the automaton as they rolled themselves to the stronghold's front entrance. Velgor then stormed the stronghold along with Ochi, who the Shade Covenant stopped from killing all of the agents inside. When Ochi expressed more confusion, Velgor explained that Crimson Dawn was just a symptom and that he would destroy the disease. He then discovered a layout of every Dawn agent within the Dominion, which Ochi and Valance noted went all the way to the top.
Following this, Velgor and his followers returned to Nexara City-world. Valance, the assassins, and the Revengers left for the Zanchal Estate while Velgor and Ochi left for the Dominion Palace.
Upon entering the Palace, they struck down all of the Royal Guards and aides in the Overlord's throne room, except for Sly Moore. Unfazed by this, Sidious pointed out Velgor should have kept one of them alive for questioning. When Velgor asked him if they were loyal, Sidious said they were loyal to strength, which Velgor showed them. However, only one of them was Crimson Dawn, meaning that Velgor's list was flawed. When Sidious questioned if his apprentice already knew that, Velgor hesitated before assuring his master that he would bring him order. Velgor and Ochi then left for the Zanchal Estate to confront Baron Espis Zanchal for his loyalty to the Dawn. Upon arriving, Velgor was thanked by a pair of hostages who the Revengers had rescued from the Baron. As he returned to his shuttle, Velgor informed Ochi that the list was a plant, which Velgor himself had deduced when Ochi could only confirm that half of the names on it were Crimson Dawn. Velgor told Ochi to find out what connected every other person on the list. That way, they would find out who planted it and why.
Hidden enemy Ochi of Bestoon.
You spoke with Romodi …and then he attacked my soldiers …as if he knew they were coming.
Uh…I did tell you…that he…he…Million hands, my ass! ―Xarn Velgor reveals Ochi's betrayal Later on the Imperator , Velgor was informed by Ochi that Romodi and his soldiers were not Crimson Dawn after all. However, Ochi warned him that Romodi did not understand the Shade Covenant and would come for Velgor someday. Stepping out of his chamber, Velgor asked Ochi what connected the false names on the list. Ochi revealed that they had all undertaken a mission for Romodi to execute the Amidalans on Aqualis. Following this development, Velgor and his team of assassins traveled to Tranchar to capture and interrogate Lieutenant Grapa about the mission. After fighting their way through legion vanguard and DT-series sentry automatons , the assassins discovered civilians being tortured by the Imperials. Gee-Ninety broke from the mission to free the civilians, prompting Velgor to destroy the automaton as punishment. Velgor went on to interrogate Grapa about her mission to kill the Amidalans.
As she claimed to Velgor that they were all dead,
Tanka blasted her in the head for revenge. Velgor then realized that the Imperials had known they were coming. When Velgor asked Valance who had sent the automatons, Valance confirmed they were from Romodi's command. Velgor then remembered that Ochi had spoken with Romodi prior to their departure. When he confronted Ochi with that fact, Ochi tried to stammer out an explanation before giving up and running away while Valance fired at him. After stealing a spare ship, Ochi escaped from Tranchar and eventually arrived on Aqualis to meet with Sabé. Velgor followed the assassin in his TIE Advanced. As soon as Ochi stepped out of his ship, Velgor revealed himself and destroyed it with his TIE. After landing in front of Ochi, Velgor revealed he had known of his allegiance to the Dawn and gave the assassin an out by commending him for leading him to Sabé, which Ochi went along with and claimed was his plan all along. As Velgor confronted Sabé, he took her Crimson Dawn badge from her and claimed that he was the Dawn.
Recruiting Sabé Does nothing scare you, handmaiden?
Why should I be scared of Xarn Velgor? ―Xarn Velgor and Sabé Following this, Velgor took Ochi, Sabé, and ZED-6-7 to the carcass of the sando aqua monster he had slain. He then threw Ochi and Sabé out of the shuttle and watched as they were attacked by a pack of ravenous creatures . Ochi attempted to assure Velgor of his loyalty, but Velgor silenced him and asked Sabé why she believed in Crimson Dawn. As she defended herself, she said that she had merely infiltrated the syndicate. Ochi fought alongside her and claimed he had done the same for the Dominion. When Velgor asked Sabé if she also intended to serve the Dominion, Sabé said she did not as long as the Dominion served chaos. Sabé went on to claim that she and Velgor wanted the same thing: order.
To this, Velgor levitated the creatures with the Synth Flux and told Sabé that if she killed one monster, a thousand more would replace it. He then explained that one would have to either make the monsters agree or deal with them all. With that, Velgor threw the creatures at a group of long-necked beasts as they sprouted from the ground. Understanding Velgor's plan, Sabé brought him to a Crimson Dawn outpost while Ochi returned to the Imperator to speak with Romodi. Upon arriving at the outpost, Sabé informed the Dawners there that they served Velgor now. To this, the Dawners attacked Velgor and were easily cut down by the Shade Lord. Velgor then had Ochi use Valance and the assassins as bait to draw the remaining Dawners and Romodi's forces to Oro Sankeria . As the Dawners and Imperials fought one another, they were suddenly wiped out by a pulse bomb, thus eliminating many of Velgor's enemies. Velgor informed Sabé that Crimson Dawn and Romodi feared him now. When he asked Sabé if she was scared, Sabé asked him why she should be scared of his true self, Xarn Velgor.
Velgor responded that Xarn Velgor was dead. But Sabé revealed that she had deduced Velgor's identity from his obsession with order, his investigation into Padmé's death, and Padmé's final words at Polis Massa. When Velgor drew his flux blade and asked Sabé what she wanted, she claimed she wanted to help him achieve order. She went on to explain that when Kast never returned to Vorath Prime to free his mother, she and Tonra secretly worked in her honor to free enslaved people and smuggle them from Vorath Prime to a colony on Gabredor III . Now, the colony was being exploited and neglected by the corrupt Governor Tauntaza , who was in league with Crimson Dawn. Sabé told Velgor that if he helped her save the refugees, he would get the chance to destroy another Crimson Dawn cell and prove that Padmé was right about him. To this, Velgor withdrew his flux blade and accepted Sabé's proposal. He then warned her that she alone was responsible for where her path took her before they silently boarded the shuttle and left for Gabredor III.
Familiar faces Your names?
K-Kitster.
«Wald.» ―Xarn Velgor, Kitster Banai, and Wald As they approached the planet, they were met by Tauntaza's flagship . When Velgor asked ZED-6-7 if the ship's crew had detected them, the automaton said they had not yet. Velgor then told him to take them to the planet's surface. Sabé suggested summoning the Imperator , but Velgor said they would first visit the colony to see if she was telling the truth. Once they landed at the colony, they stepped out of the ship as ZED-6-7 introduced Velgor to the refugees and asked to see their protocol automaton . Since their protocol automaton had long broken down, ZED-6-7 instead spoke with their astromech automaton Em-Sevenbee . The automaton and two of the refugees then confirmed Sabé's story. The pair of refugees then showed off a terrestrial vehicle they had built, inspired by their earlier lives on Vorath Prime. When Velgor asked the refugees for their names, they introduced themselves as Kitster and Wald; Kast's childhood friends.
When Kitster asked Velgor if they could do anything for him, Velgor told them they could do nothing. He then sensed that the surrounding land was disturbed, to which ZED-6-7 explained that it was currently suffering from drought. When another refugee claimed things would get better once the clouds came back, Velgor said they would only get worse as they were attacked by a pack of clawed beasts. While Velgor and Sabé fought off the creatures, a few of them surrounded Kitster and Wald. Velgor then leapt in and cut them down, saving the refugees. Kitster thanked Velgor as the Shade Lord silently walked away. Sabé then asked Velgor if he had seen enough and told him it was time to take out Tauntaza. However, Velgor told Sabé she was not ready, since the refugees had ignored her order to stay back and put themselves in danger. He then told her that if she wanted them to live, they would have to obey her.
Afterwards, Velgor had Sabé don a set of Dominion equipment and recruited her into the Dominion Military under the rank of Lieutenant Commander, granting her authority over all the forces he gathered.
He then ordered an Dominion officer to open a channel to the Imperator . When the officer reported that Tauntaza's ship was blocking their communications, Velgor ordered for his shuttle to be prepared. He and his team then traveled to the governor's facility and proceeded to fight their way towards the governor. After cutting down a few repurposed battle automatons, Velgor was confronted by Tauntaza, who briefly subdued the Shade Lord with a life-draining weapon. Velgor's team was then attacked by more battle automatons and a few Crimson Dawn assassins. Once Velgor recovered, he Flux-choked Ochi for allowing the governor to escape. Ochi explained that he was assisting Velgor, to which Velgor asked Ochi if he thought Tauntaza's weapon could destroy him. Sabé then told Velgor that it might have, prompting Velgor to release Ochi and meet with one of the weapon's designers, Doctor Aira . Aira explained that the technology had been used to raze the planet Karolia and expressed remorse over not having known what the technology would be used for. As Sabé reassured the rest of the regretful designers, Velgor seized Ochi's speeder and left to find the governor.
Catching up to Tauntaza and her escorts, Velgor blocked a blast from one of her battle automatons before reaching her at her escape ship. As the ship took off, Velgor used the Synth Flux to hold it and destroy its interior controls. However, the ship's powerful engines ultimately allowed the governor to make her escape. Using a pair of Imperials as a diversion, Velgor boarded Tauntaza's flagship and fought off more battle automatons and Imperials before confronting the one in charge. When Velgor asked where Tauntaza was, the Dominion claimed there had been a misunderstanding and recommended that the Shade Covenant reconsider his "hostile stance". Velgor then had the ship's crew open a channel to his own ship and ordered Drax to lock onto the governor's flagship. After giving Drax the order to destroy the ship unless countermanded, Velgor asked the Dominion again for the governor's location. Upon learning the location, Velgor returned to his shuttle and flew toward the facility where she was hiding, only to be met by a sandstorm generated by the facility.
Despite the pilots' warnings, Velgor told them to continue approaching the facility while he held the ship together with the Synth Flux. A lightning bolt then struck the ship, causing it to crash into the forest. Once Velgor stepped out of the wreckage, he was met by Ochi, who he asked for Sabé's location. Ochi reported that the storm had taken her and a colonist claimed that the storm would kill them all. When Velgor asked the colonist if they had any ships, he said the only ship that had not been confiscated or destroyed was Kitster's racer. When the colonist said that only Kitster could drive it, Velgor said they would see as he approached the racer.
ZED-6-7 told Velgor that he could not trace Sabé or the other colonists, to which Velgor told the automaton he would command his assistance if he required it. Faced with numerous flashbacks to his past, Velgor then boarded the racer and sped off into the storm. While deflecting debris with his flux blade, Velgor located Kitster on the ground alongside a large tank. Landing next to him, Velgor asked Kitster where Sabé was. Kitster explained that she was trapped beneath the tank and that he had been trying to move it. Once Velgor lifted the tank off of Sabé, the three of them were overwhelmed by the surrounding storm. Velgor then contacted Drax and ordered him to have the Imperator fire on his location.
When Drax warned that would incinerate everyone and everything within range, Velgor choked him with the Synth Flux, prompting him to open fire. The resulting bombardment destroyed the tank and dissipated the storm. After cutting himself out from the remains of the tank, Velgor took Sabé from Kitster and carried her into the distance.
Eventually, the three of them returned to the colonists and Velgor ordered Drax to open fire on Tauntaza's facility. However, the blasts were deflected by a shield, which ZED-6-7 explained was drawing energy from the planet. Despite Ochi's doubts, Velgor remained certain the fortress possessed a weakness and, when ZED-6-7 revealed that the facility's underside was unshielded, took Em-Sevenbee with him to the facility. While he defeated more monsters on his way there, Velgor placed his flux blade within the astromech's dome. Once they arrived at the facility, he had the automaton fire the flux blade upwards into the unshielded underbelly. Velgor then used the Synth Flux to guide the flux blade through the facility, disabling the weapon and carving an entrance for himself. With the shield down, Velgor entered the facility and struck down Tauntaza's soldiers.
However, he was then met by Tauntaza herself, wearing a suit of powerful armor. Tauntaza used the suit's arm cannon to fire at Velgor. As he blocked the beam with his flux blade, Velgor told the governor she could not contain the power she was wielding. To this, Tauntaza accused Velgor of having no control over his own power before he destroyed her arm cannon. Tauntaza then flew away as the facility exploded, leaving Velgor severely injured without any power left in his suit. Sabé soon found Velgor and begrudgingly restored his suit's power, allowing them to walk back to the colony. Once they returned, they learned from ZED-6-7 that Tauntaza had been receiving her orders from the Overlord himself. When Velgor asked Sabé why she was smiling, she said she now knew why she had saved him.
Returning to his fortress, Velgor healed in his bacta tank, but broke it in anger upon remembering Sabé's words about the good still in him.
Later, Velgor boarded the Imperator and was met by Valance, Haydenn, and the Revengers. Velgor chastised them for being early, to which Valance demanded to know why they were there. Velgor then used the Synth Flux to fix Valance's collar, saying he expected those who served him to be held to a certain standard. Haydenn claimed that Valance had the team operating at peak efficiency. To this, Velgor said they would get a chance to prove their loyalty and advised Valance to channel his energies in a less self-destructive manner. When Valance asked what their mission was, Drax explained that they had been selected to protect a medicine shipment to Bestine IV from Crimson Dawn. Velgor warned the mercenaries not to disappoint him as he would be watching before they left for their mission.
Velgor then met with Sabé and asked her about Tauntaza's location. When she said Ochi was searching for the governor, Velgor asked her what she advised they do. To this, Sabé told Velgor he should do what he always did: directly confront the problem.
Return to Skako Minor Follow that speeder. Infiltrate their organization. Find Jul Tambor. I will take care of the rest. ―Xarn Velgor, to Sabé Following Sabé's counsel, Velgor and Sabé arrived at the Dominion Palace on Nexara City-world aboard a Lambda
-class shuttle to confront Sidious. As the two were unexpected, one of Draven Nul's advisors tried to stop the Dark Lord, but Velgor only reacted by throwing the advisor against a wall before making his way into the Overlord's throne room. Although his two present Royal Guards pointed their
Flux pikes at Velgor, Sidious reacted by calmly asking Velgor what his heart told him. Velgor proceeded to hold Sabé in the air with the Synth Flux as Sidious laughed and claimed to her that she had only reminded Velgor, who was remembering moments of his past at the same time, of his weakness. Dropping her, Velgor refused to assist Sabé as the Overlord had his Royal Guards attempt to destroy her. Once she outwitted them in combat, Sidious killed the guards in anger and, seeing her worth as an agent, allowed Sabé to continue working with Velgor.
As they returned to their shuttle, Velgor and Sabé were informed by ZED-6-7 of their next mission.
Jul Tambor , the grandson of Wat Tambor, was working to overthrow the pro-Dominion regime on Skako Minor through a nationalist uprising . According to Dominion intelligence, Tambor had also enlisted pirates to blockade the planet and stockpile technology to be used against the Dominion. As such, their mission was to end the Skakoan's uprising and restore Dominion control over Skako Minor.
In truth, Tambor had known he would have to face Velgor as part of his crusade. Over the course of the last six years, the Skakoan had worked to collect and reassemble automatons who had been killed by Velgor, thereby allowing him to analyze their databanks to study Velgor's moves. Amongst the automatons he reassembled were B1 battle automatons, B2 super battle automatons, and Gee-Ninety.
Tambor was very confident that he would be able to kill Velgor.
Upon reaching the planet, Velgor and Sabé discovered a crashed commercial transport from Corellia . ZED-6-7 explained that the ship had been boarded and those inside were killed by the planet's poisonous atmosphere. As they traversed the terrain, Velgor killed a pack of cliff worms , saving a group of pirates. When he noticed a pile of corpses on the ground, he struck down the pirates, while Sabé hesitated and allowed one of them to escape. When Velgor asked her if the pirates deserved mercy, Sabé admitted she didn't know who did anymore. Velgor reminded her that she was his shadow now and she said they would need one of the pirates alive in order to locate Tambor. To this, Velgor instructed Sabé to follow the surviving pirate and find Tambor while he took care of the rest.
Confronting Valance Beilert Valance…you have proven to be a disappointment. A betrayal that will not be tolerated. ―Xarn Velgor Upon learning that Valance had betrayed the Dominion, Velgor boarded the Righteous Fist and had Haydenn accompany him as he approached the surface of Bestine IV in his shuttle. Once they landed, Velgor and Haydenn confronted Valance and the rest of the Edgehawk s crew. Drawing his flux blade, Velgor told Valance that his desertion would not be tolerated.
Charging into battle, Valance told Velgor that he regretted ever serving the Dominion. As they fought, Velgor took note of Valance's power, which he regarded as proof Valance was a waste of potential. Once he was beaten into submission, Valance said that the planet's people would still be free of the Dominion. However, Velgor revealed that Valance had doomed them as his forces bombed the locals, as well as his troops.
Ignoring Valance's chastisation of his lack of care for his own, Velgor prepared to strike Valance down, but Haydenn insisted on finishing the job herself to make up for her previous failures, which Velgor allowed. Valance tried to convince Haydenn to leave the Dominion with him, but Haydenn blasted him in the face and sent him falling over the edge of the cliff. Before leaving the planet, Velgor contacted Haydenn and ordered her to send in a group of Imperials to hunt Valance down in case he survived. To accomplish this task, Velgor personally selected Inferno Squad Unmasking the Dawn I did not train this woman. But perhaps…the one who trained her. If you had found this Qi'ra in a royal court, I would think Tyranus had a hand in this. But she wallows in the mud of the criminal syndicates, so the more likely answer is… Maul ―Draven Nul and Xarn Velgor As Sabé searched for Jul Tambor,
Velgor was called to the Dominion Palace by Sidious. Upon meeting with his master, Velgor was informed by Sidious that the Shade Covenant had an enemy, claiming that the recent attacks were part of a coordinated campaign of diversion. Sidious then called in ISB Director Sliro Barsha to report on his current investigation. Barsha reported that his division within the ISB —the undercover criminal syndicate Zerek Besh —had learned of a rumor that the major crime syndicates were vying to obtain the Hutts' long-standing arrangement with the Dominion. However, the rumor was coming from a single source: Crimson Dawn. As they walked through the Palace halls, Velgor agreed that Crimson Dawn had orchestrated the Syndicate War but pondered how deep its influence went.
Sidious went on to state that they had orchestrated the other attacks as well, saying that Crimson Dawn had targeted the Shade Covenant. He then asked Velgor about the syndicate's leader, Qi'ra, who Velgor fought and failed to acquire Corbin from. Velgor told Sidious that while Qi'ra had not been a true threat to him, she was trained in Teräs Käsi and possessed a fighting style which reminded Velgor of his master's. From this, Sidious and Velgor deduced that, since Qi'ra wallowed in the criminal underworld, she must have been trained by Maul. Sidious then concluded that Maul must have trained Qi'ra to use her as an instrument of revenge and went on to ponder what else he had taught her. With the Shade Covenant aware of Crimson Dawn's role in recent events, an alert was sent out the rest of the Dominion Military to track down the Vermillion , which made Qi'ra order her assets in the Dominion and other major factions to sow chaos across the stellar realm to further distract the Shade Covenant. With the Screaming Key they had stolen from Velgor, the Knights of Ren, meanwhile, traveled to a location known as the deep flux hellscape a location unknown to Velgor and Sidious, alongside " the Archivist " on orders from Qi'ra to retrieve an artifact known as the Fermata Cage Dealing with Zerek Besh After Director Barsha and Zerek Besh failed to bring in a single member of the Free Worlds Coalition, Velgor confronted him on the ISB space station Derosha Six . There Barsha tried to appeal to Velgor by announcing the successes of Zerek Besh, but Velgor countered that, because of the criminal syndicate's inability to bring in any rebels, Zerek Besh and Barsha by extension were failures. Barsha continued to boast about the size of the network the ISB had created under Zerek Besh, and insulted Velgor about his and the Dominion army's failures on Kryos. In his anger, Velgor destroyed a glass display behind him using the force, and surrounded Barsha in the shards. Forced to ground, Barsha pleaded with Velgor, saying that he was close. Velgor simply replied that he needed to find rebels then.
Shortly after his departure, thiefs Kay and Riko Vess were able to steal access codes to Director Barsha's palace vault on Canto Bight . From there,
Kay Vess' crew working under Barsha's brother Jaylen Vrax were able to steal a secret codex Barsha had that detailed sensitive information about almost all ranking Dominion officers. Vrax contacted Velgor and informed him of the database and his intentions to take control of Zerek Besh. They made a deal, in exchange for the codex and the coordinates to a Coalition base on Akiva , Vrax would be given Zerek Besh. Later, Velgor ordered Barsha onto the bridge of the Revelator , where he condemned Barsha for letting such critical Dominion intelligence go. Velgor then looked to Vrax and said the Dominion had accepted his terms. He ended the call and gave up Barsha to the mercy of Vrax and his associates.
Finding the Fermata Cage Velgor was amongst Draven Nul's tools in the crusade to root out Dawn agents across the Dominion. During his effort to destroy the Dawn, Velgor bisected a traitorous legion vanguard in an industrial environment.
At one point, Velgor nevertheless returned to his castle on Mustafar, where he was called by the Eye of Webbish Bog. Upon entering the Eye's cave, the Eye showed Velgor a moment from the Gene Wars, where he, Vorn, and their gen-soldiers had fought B1 battle automatons, and glimpses of the future, including one of him being confronted by Amidala's surviving handmaidens. Velgor then realized that he had been lured there as a test before being attacked by Mustafarians. After killing them, Velgor claimed that the Eye had wasted his time with puzzles. To this, the Eye explained that the visions were warnings for the end of everything. When Velgor asked why he should care, the Eye reminded Velgor that he was a part of everything, but, if he refused to accept this, he could not be helped. Just as the Eye had shown him,
Velgor, with his helmet slightly damaged, returned to his fortress and, after sensing a great disturbance in the deep flux, was contacted by Sidious to discuss the disturbance.
Informed by Sidious that Qi'ra claimed to possess the Fermata Cage, Velgor was ordered to find the artifact and kill anyone who knew of it.
Sidious also told Velgor about Qi'ra's central claim about the artifact: it contained an ancient Shade Lord who was so powerful they had needed to be locked away.
In truth, no such Shade Lord existed; Qi'ra simply needed a claim that would draw both Shade Covenant to the Cage, allowing her to lock them both into it and free the stellar realm from their rule.
Her initial effort to open the cage, however, had resulted in the Knights of Ren abandoning Crimson Dawn out anger at being hurt in the attempt and out of fear of the ripple it sent out.
Velgor made his way to the site of the disturbance, where he only found wreckage from the experiment. Although he was certain it was the origin of the disturbance, he deduced, due to a lack of bodies, that no Shade Lord had been released. Velgor then returned to the Imperator and reported his findings to Sidious via its holoprojection chamber.
Velgor asked Sidious if there truly was an ancient Shade Covenant within the Cage, which Sidious explained was possible: built by Darth Momin himself, the Cage could imprison people and places outside of time and had never been used lightly.
Sidious further warned Velgor that, because of the urge within every Shade Covenant to dominate others, the mere possibility was the greatest danger to everything they had built together. Amid their conversation, the two suddenly felt the Cage begin to open once more as the Archivist and her assistant, archaeologist Kho Phon Farrus , tried to use Ascendant
-designed
technology to focus nearby deep flux energy into it. Although Farrus shut off their technology as the Cage began to leach life energy from each of them, the experiment had carried on for long enough that Sidious was able to track the disturbance to the planet they were on. With this, Sidious sent Velgor to retrieve the Cage and destroy those present.
The Orphans [Xarn Velgor] radiates a particular, dark energy. But he is also other, like whatever drives him is completely removed from motivations experienced by other beings. Do you know the feeling you get when you hear a story about an animal eating its own young? That's what being in Xarn Velgor's presence felt like to me. The first time I saw Xarn Velgor was when he came to kill me. He failed. I think that was rare for him. As a general rule, when Xarn Velgor tried to kill something…it died. ―The Archivist Once the Imperator arrived at the planet,
Velgor, intending to handle the situation on his own, ordered Drax to prepare his Lambda
-class T-4a shuttle and landed at the seemingly-deserted site with two legion vanguard.
In the vicinity of the artifact, where he could not single out the presence of the supposed ancient Shade Covenant, Velgor sensed a great deal of deep flux power from the Fermata Cage.
Velgor discovered the Archivist and Kho Phon Farrus trying to hide beneath the remains of their machine.
In Velgor's presence, the Archivist found that the Shade Lord radiated a dark energy.
While Farrus, having been warned of Velgor by their rival Lyra Venn, quickly surrendered, the Archivist refused, to which Velgor began to choke the agents with the Synth Flux. However, he was interrupted by the arrival of Chanath Cha and her Orphans , consisting of Sear Ladybright , and Imara Vex . When Cha asked Velgor if he remembered her, Velgor simply stated that he would kill her as he bared his flux blade.
Cha went on to introduce herself, explaining that Velgor had slaughtered her family and that she had spent every day since then plotting her revenge. Velgor quickly forgot her name once more before the Orphans attacked. After being electrocuted by Ladybright and burned by one of Cha's acid bombs, Velgor pushed the Orphans back and walked into the ocean to recover. Once he did, he returned to the shore and saw Ladybright, Vex, Pharrus and the Archivist fleeing for the Siroto . When Velgor mentioned Qi'ra's death to Cha and Sear, he sensed their confusion and lack of fear, indicating that Qi'ra was still alive. As he fought Cha and Sear, he chastised them for their arrogance and prepared to strike Cha down, only for Vex to snipe off one of his hands, prompting him to tip over the Siroto with the Synth Flux and throw Vex into the forest. With Vex severely injured, Ladybright, Pharrus and the Archivist fled in the Siroto before returning to assist Cha and Sear. However, the Siroto then turned back around and left the planet, much to Cha's relief. With Sear and Vex defeated, Velgor twisted Cha's introduction of herself by re-introducing himself and stabbed her through the chest.
With Cha dead, Velgor left the planet to find Qi'ra and reunited with his master. Both traveled a dark, smoky location, where Sidious asked Velgor if he actually felt the presence of an ancient Shade Lord in the Cage. With Velgor admitting he only felt intense deep flux power from it, he engaged Sidious in a sparring duel, the first they had engaged in in some time, at his master's request, allowing Sidious to explain how he would enforce the Rule of Two if another Shade Lord indeed existed. After Sidious pushed back Velgor with a final strike and declared the duel over, Velgor made one last rush at his master before stopping when his master repeated himself. Feeling they had no choice but to follow the ripple in the Synth Flux caused by the next Cage opening, Sidious took the moment to admit Qi'ra would be missed as he and Velgor looked across the location.
Qi'ra attempted to sure up her chances by reaching out the Knights of Ren, reminding them that they had attacked Velgor's fortress and thus would be hunted by the Shade Covenant if they did not help her destroy Velgor and Draven Nul.
Around the same time, the Spark Eternal artificial intelligence, created thousands of years prior for use against the Shade Covenant by the Ascendant cult, resolved to hunt down Velgor. The AI had taken over Lyra Venn's body to carry out its mission, further collecting 0-0-0 and BT-1 to help in its quest. When Lyra Venn used a virus she had installed into the two automatons to regain control of her body, the Spark introduced, much to Lyra Venn's horror, their Shade Covenant hunting mission against Velgor.
By possessing Lyra Venn, it had access to her memories of Velgor and saw how she considered the Shade Lord a monster, but the AI believed itself ready to take on the Shade Lord and his master. Because Lyra Venn had once outmaneuvered Velgor, the overconfident Spark thought itself skilled enough to engage him in one-on-one combat.
In fact, Qi'ra contacted the Spark to request its help in destroying the Shade Covenant.
Destroying the Dawn You wish to board the station, Lord Velgor. I sense your desire . To possibly meet an ancient Shade Covenant, one so dangerous they were locked away in such a powerful prison as the Fermata Cage . To feel their power. To test yourself against it. And then… to destroy them. Or even… to be destroyed . I desire it too, Lord Velgor. But it is a foolish desire. And I am not a fool. ―Draven Nul, to Xarn Velgor The Archivist moved the Fermata Cage and the equipment needed to finally open it to an ancient Amaxine Space Station in the Outer Rim, where the Cage could feed off the plant life and deep flux energy left behind by the ancient Drengir beings.
Sensing the disturbance in the Synth Flux caused by the opening of the Cage, Sidious and Velgor, just as Sidious said they would need to in the name of enforcing the Rule of Two, followed the disturbance aboard the Imperator with a fleet of Dominion Cruisers at their back.
The Shade Lords and their forces reached the station shortly into the opening process. Overlooking the station from the Imperator s bridge, Velgor found himself with overwhelming desire to board the station and meet the possible Shade Covenant, hoping to test himself against that Shade Lord's power no matter if he survived the encounter. Able to sense his apprentice's wish, Sidious felt the same but declared it a foolish desire and ordered the Imperator to destroy the station.
Before the Imperator could fire, the warship's crew detected an entire enemy fleet emerging from voidspace. While Velgor initially assumed it was the Free Worlds Coalition, the force was Qi'ra's secret Dawn Fleet which targeted the Imperator s long-range weapons, thereby forcing the Imperator to divert power to its shield batteries and delay the Overlord's order to destroy the station. With his master unwilling to take any chances, Velgor boarded a Lambda
-class T-4a shuttle with Sidious and
an escort of death troopers and Overlord's Royal Guards, flying through the space battle under the escort of crimson TIE interceptors . As their troops engaged with Dawn soldiers who had set up a parameter in the hangar, Sidious and Velgor disembarked from their shuttle into the station, which was filled with deep flux energy that Sidious could tell was not all related to the Fermata Cage. Unsure of what they would find, the two Shade Lords led the charge with the mission to totally destroy the Dawn.
Leading from the front, Velgor and Sidious fought their way through the station. As the two Shade Covenant could deflect pulse rifle bolts with their flux blades, Dawn Squads Two and Three attempted to engage Sidious and Velgor with electrified melee weapons, but both Shade Covenant effortlessly fought through the guards. Still, Velgor toyed with one soldier until Sidious ordered him to stop and simply kill them. As they journeyed closer to the Cage, they were confronted by the Spark Eternal-possessed Lyra Venn, who mocked the two Shade Covenant. Velgor quickly deduced Lyra Venn was under some kind of possession and stayed behind to deal with her while Sidious carried on. Believing she could weaken him, the overconfident Spark engaged Velgor with the cortosis alloy-based Null Blade , which would temporarily shut down flux blades.
To the Spark, the battle represented a chance to finally fulfill the dream of its long-dead creator,
Miril , by destroying the Shade Covenant Order, even if the battle proved too much for Lyra Venn's body.
The blades of Velgor and the possessed Lyra Venn clashed several times, thereby shorting out the Shade Lord's flux blade.
While the Spark at that moment thought it was ready to destroy Velgor and jumped at him,
Velgor effortlessly excised the Spark from Lyra Venn's body with a Flux push as a group of new arrivals watched: Lyra Venn's former lover Sana Starros had gathered a of Lyra Venn's friends and loved ones to rescue her from the Spark.
Having abandoned the Dawn in fear after their run in with Velgor,
Farrus was also among the group.
Unknown to Velgor, who declared the Spark to be a weak enemy, he had effectively ended the dream of the Ascendant cult by defeating its main weapon without much effort. After a terrified Lyra Venn awoke under Velgor and escaped as the opening Cage sent out a ripple, Velgor elected to return to his master's side rather than hunt down his former ally.
With Velgor out of sight, the Spark attempted to possess Lyra Venn once more, but the intervention of Farrus, Starros, and another one of Lyra Venn's ex-lovers, Magna Tolvan, saved her.
Velgor took his place at an eager Sidious's side as the Cage finally opened. However, only a small disc without any connection to the Synth Flux fell out: inside, there had been no secret Shade Lord after all, and Sidious believed the disc was nothing important. Unknown to both, the disc was actually a automaton locked away by the ancient Shade Covenant.
Watching from afar aboard each of their starships, the Archivist and Qi'ra remotely re-activated the Cage, encasing Velgor and Sidious in a time-lock . Qi'ra intended to recover the Cage with the two Shade Covenant trapped inside and throw their prison into a star. However, it was at that moment that cannon shots from beyond the space battle suddenly broke into the station and destroyed the Cage: while it remained physically together, the Cage's time-lock collapsed, freeing the two Shade Covenant from its grasp but sending a Flux Wave out across the stellar realm.
The cannon shots had come from the Night Buzzard , personal transport of the Knights of Ren: although Ren had watched Velgor and Sidious fall victim to Qi'ra's plan from a distance, he believed Sidious would ultimately find his way out of the Cage and thus believed the only way to save his crew was to align themselves with the Shade Covenant.
The wave sent out by the collapse of the Cage wiped out much of the Dawn Fleet, giving the Imperials a chance to wipe out the Dawn forces as the escaping Qi'ra gave a retreat order. As they collected the destroyed but intact Fermata Cage, Sidious and Velgor sensed a great rending in the Synth Flux sent out by its collapse, with Sidious admitting it made his grasp on the Synth Flux tenuous. Sidious and Velgor then came face-to-face with the Knights of Ren, who revealed themselves in return for mercy. Sidious, however, responded with a blast of Flux lightning to punish the Knights, promising that whatever was left of the group would be put to excellent use. Still, Sidious declared the Knights' future a small matter and ordered Velgor to depart with him. In the aftermath of the Crimson Dawn war, the syndicate was shattered while the defeated Qi'ra fled into hiding.
The effects of the Synth Flux Wave varied at different times: Flux-sensitives could find themselves with a limited connection to the Synth Flux at one time but could wield great power when the Wave returned. Velgor came to fear the great energy provided by the Synth Flux Wave because he could not control it.
Fighting the past and the Synth Flux Reunion with the Handmaidens As the Synth Flux Wave vanished for the time being,
Velgor returned to the Imperator and meditated, reflecting on Malkor's words that only a Shade Covenant dealt in absolutes and declaring that no one in the stellar realm should forget his allegiance. Afterward, Velgor had Drax contact the recently-returned Ochi, who had returned to the Imperator with Sabé, to bring Sabé into a room aboard the ship. Within, Velgor had also summoned Sabé's recently assigned personal guards, legion vanguard KA-336 and KV-222 ; both troopers had broken protocol by openly speaking about her classified assignment, with Velgor demanding the woman before him kill them both with a nearby E-11 pulse rifle rifle as punishment. In truth, Velgor suspected that the woman was not Sabé, but another of Amidala's surviving handmaidens, and was testing that theory. Velgor also created a struggle when he urged the troopers to also run for the pulse rifle. "Sabé" grabbed it first and fired at them, injuring both but leaving them alive. This confirmed Velgor's suspicion that this was not Sabé and instead another handmaiden,
Dormé . Lifted into the air via a Flux choke, Dormé claimed that Sabé wouldn't have killed them, which Velgor declared meant Dormé did not know Sabé at all.
Upon realizing that Ochi had helped her get inside as part of a scheme get rid of Sabé, whom Ochi saw as a rival, Velgor threw Ochi into a wall and told Dormé that Sabé served him willingly. Dormé denied Velgor's claim, saying Sabé was the best of them. To this, Velgor brought her to a hangar and told her she should follow Sabé's lead by claiming the same power. When Ochi advised against this, Velgor tossed him aside, saying he simply needed her to serve his purpose. Dormé stated once again that neither she nor Sabé would serve Velgor. Velgor then said they would see before he took her aboard a Lambda
-class T-4a shuttle and traveled to
the planet Fedalle Shortly after, Dormé's fellow handmaidens Eirtaé Saché , and Rabé Tonsort arrived on the planet. When Dormé insisted they go after Sabé instead, the handmaidens told her they wouldn't leave her as they engaged Velgor in combat. Despite their coordination, the handmaidens were subdued by Velgor, who gave them one final chance to join him. However, Saché revealed that Eirtaé had seized codes from Velgor's suit during the fight, allowing the handmaidens to complete Dormé's sabotage of the Imperator . Before they could destroy Velgor's flagship, which would kill thousands in the name of rescuing Sabé and defeating Velgor, Velgor declared they were just as bloodthirsty as he needed them to be to join him. However, he was contacted by Drax, who showed him a transmission from Tambor.
After arriving on the planet Brentaal IV , Sabé had confronted Tambor while he was helping out a village of exiled Skakoans, urging him not to go up against Velgor and declaring that the Shade Lord had only grown more dangerous since her own failed efforts on Polis Massa. She was certain that Tambor's efforts against Velgor would fail and that the Shade Lord would slaughter the hundreds of Skakoans on Brentaal IV in retaliation.
Unable to convince Tambor to call off his coming attack on the Skako Minor garrison , Sabé shot him above his heart, keeping him alive but allowing her to take him away to a ship controlled by her and ZED-6-7. Aboard, Tambor listened to her reasoning about the danger Velgor posed and declared that it was time to kill the Shade Lord, capturing Sabé after his rebuilt automatons reached the starship. After showing Sabé video footage of Velgor, Tambor sent his hologram to Velgor, in which he revealed her capture and baited him to come after him.
While fully aware of the Shade Lord's ruthlessness and not doubting Velgor's willingness to slaughter his refugee camp in retaliation, the Skakoan had come to believe that Sabé was Velgor's weakness, which would finally give him an advantage over the Dark Lord. On Fedalle, realizing they would need Velgor and his forces to stage a daring rescue, the handmaidens reluctantly joined Velgor to go after Tambor, much to Ochi's annoyance. The group returned returned to the Imperator aboard his Lambda shuttle and eventually left for Brentaal IV.
Corrupting Sabé The other handmaidens fled . But you are here Stronger . And you will become stronger still They only left … because I kept my secret Your secret. If I'd told them what really happened… to Xarn Velgor … they would never have escaped. I know… my sisters… will always think of me with grief … and they will always wonder what happened here… but they will go back to their lives … and their loves . This way… only one of us has to lose their soul ―Xarn Velgor and Sabé after the fall of Jul Tambor Upon reaching Brentaal IV via a Lambda
-class shuttle with the handmaidens and ZED-6-7, Velgor reflected on his last meeting with Amidala on Mustafar and how he wished it had gone, privately declaring to himself he would finally have his idealized outcome.
He also saw it as a way to have his idealized version of the duel on Bespin, privately comparing the situation to him reaching out to Joren Kast.
Entering a building on-world, Velgor and the handmaidens were met by Moore, who explained with Velgor how each of the handmaidens could benefit from serving the Dominion and the Shade Lord. When none of the four agreed to his demands, he telekinetically threw all four outside through an opening created by Tambor's forces, who had tracked down the Shade Lord and his reluctant allies, and forced them to fight the B1 battle automaton horde alone. To Velgor, the battle was a chance for the handmaidens to earn their places at his side and show them their own power. Overlooking their victory, Velgor remained silent as Moore declared it an example of their skills, which she promised would only grow under Velgor's service.
Shortly thereafter, Eirtaé discovered from one of the B1's memory banks that Tambor's command ship, in which she learned Sabé was being held, was three klicks to the south of their position. However, Tambor, ignoring Sabé's continued pleas that Velgor would wipe out his refugee camp if he continued, sent three platoons of B1s and a Low Altitude Assault Transport/infantry to attack a civilian village , hoping it would trap his enemies into the village's canyon and let him escape with Sabé. Both Velgor and Moore understood the attack was a trap, with Velgor declaring it an example of the weakness that the handmaidens would need to overcome. When the handmaidens demanded that Velgor assist them in aiding the populace, Velgor elected to use them as a distraction, effectively turning them into his pawns so he could get to and corrupt Sabé, and teach them another lesson: as they fought the B1 horde, he could slip past the automatons' lines, leaving the handmaidens to seize their true potential against a major enemy. Riding into battle on speeder bikes, Velgor cut down the gunship but left the handmaidens to the automatons, cutting into Tambor's ship to save Sabé and confront the revolutionary.
Inside Tambor's ship, Velgor took position in front of Sabé's cage but was met by the rebuilt Gee-Ninety, who fired upon the Dark Lord before releasing magnetic spheres. Having deduced that Velgor would attack with brute force, Tambor and the automaton had created the spheres specifically to immobilize him via electrocution. However, the handmaidens then arrived and used Eirtaé's own magnetic devices to force down the spheres, although Tambor and Gee-Ninety managed to flee by detaching the room from the rest of the ship while Velgor recovered. The Shade Lord maintained that he had not betrayed the handmaidens, instead declaring that he had granted them a chance to claim their true power, before Flux choking Eirtaé and Dormé against the wall. Both had just released Sabé from her cage and begged her to leave Velgor's service, but Velgor offered her a chance at creating order instead of allowing the innocent-attacking Tambor to run free. As he made the offer and the handmaidens begged Sabé to turn back, Velgor remembered his lived and idealized versions of the meeting with Amidala on Mustafar and with his son on Bespin.
Sabé almost took Velgor's hand, but was tackled and pulled out of the room by Dormé while Eirtaé and Saché provided covering fire against Velgor. Velgor drew his flux blade and pursued the handmaidens outside, only for the group to take Sabé into the air with Eirtaé's antigravity devices to an overhead Star Commuter 2000 as Velgor watched on. The ship was in fact being piloted by Ochi, who had made a deal with the handmaidens that they would convince Sabé to abandon Velgor to make sure he would never see any of them again. However, Sabé believed her handmaiden sisters did not have the tools needed to put a stop to Tambor's campaign, deciding the only way to save the civilians Tambor was on the cusp of attacking was to join Velgor's violent crusade. She also decided that, by staying with Velgor, she could continue to bring about Amidala's declaration that good existed in Xarn Velgor. Bidding her fellow handmaidens goodbye, Sabé detached her antigrav device to fall back to Velgor, who guided her to the ground with the Synth Flux. While a force of legion vanguard had arrived via a Lambda
-class T-4a shuttle and were firing upon the escaping Star Commuter, Velgor called their assault off, declaring that the incident was over before walking back to the shuttle with Sabé at his side.
The following day, returning to the Imperator and receiving an update on Tambor's gunships, Velgor made it clear to Drax that the campaign against Tambor was under Sabé's command. Ordering her own shuttle be prepared for departure, Sabé enginneered the downfall of Tambor without even killing the Skakoan; contacting the revolutionary, she claimed that twelve Skakoan refugees, whom were actually in the same room as her, were imprisoned with Velgor and his forces in the abandoned village of Kantiema , which Tambor quickly bombed . Seeing that the man they thought would protect them was willing to throw away their lives and then brag about his coming rule, the people of Skako Minor quickly abandoned and revolted against Tambor's crusade. With that, the Dominion granted all his refugees amnesty and began an armistice process on Skako Minor. After Tambor's automaton army was wiped out , Sabé reported her success to Velgor, who meditated and accepted her rational for not killing Tambor, as doing so would have made the Skakoan a martyr and cost hundreds of lives. Still, Velgor felt the need to correct her by claiming they were bringing order, not mere peace.
He also inquired to her about how she felt about the power at her fingers, telling her she would continue to grow stronger as she remained in his service. However, Sabé remained convinced that her fellow handmaidens, dismissed by Velgor as weak, had only left because they did not know he was Xarn Velgor. Before leaving the room, Sabé declared it meant she was the only one present who had to lose their soul by staying under his command. Feeling a pain and losing control of the Synth Flux as the Synth Flux Wave returned,
Velgor declared to himself that Sabé was wrong in thinking she was the only one who had to lose their soul in return for the future they were working towards.
Velgor was unable to control the Synth Flux as his powers began to tear apart the Imperator , thereby presenting another threat to the Dominion: himself.
Facing the Synth Flux Wave Hate … leads to suffering … or power . You must choose . This is your last chance , handmaiden. I am more powerful than I have ever been. But I am not… in control . This is not a threat . It is simply a fact . The next time you defy me… I will kill you. Now.
Choose . It should not be hard . You already know what you desire . You saw it in your own vision . All you have to do now… is take it.
Hate leads to suffering … or power . But,
Anakin … how can I choose … if I refuse to hate ―Xarn Velgor is rebuked by Sabé Velgor's fear at his out-of-control abilities could be felt by Sidious all the way away on Nexara City-world, where the Shade Covenant Master was delighting in the uncontrolled powers afforded to him by the Synth Flux Wave. Through the Synth Flux, Sidious had reached out to Velgor to speak of the great power but, noticing the fear, urged Velgor to accept his true self. Velgor managed to exit his chamber but still failed to control the Synth Flux, crumbling parts of the Imperator around him as Sabé, who quickly understood something had gone wrong with Velgor's Flux abilities, marshalled nearby Dominion officers and legion vanguard away from the out-of-control Shade Lord. Velgor's powers began to breach the Imperator s hull in the hallway just as Sabé cleared the room. Despite the objections of one of the officers, Sabé sealed off the hallway from the rest of the ship, leaving Velgor liable to be sucked out into the vacuum of space. She attempted to contact Velgor that the inconvenience would only last a brief moment, but she was Flux choked by Velgor as Sidious spoke in his mind: Sidious offered that fear paved the way for anger and hate, but it would be up to Velgor whether he chose to turn that hatred into either power or suffering.
As Sidious spoke to him, Velgor was thrown out into the vacuum of space. Velgor reached out towards his flagship but only managed in carving a line of destruction in the Imperator s hull. However, a motionless Velgor was soon found by Sabé aboard a Lambda
-class T-4a shuttle
, from which she deployed two Prowler 1000 seeker automatons to collect the Shade Lord and bring him aboard. Although Sabé asked him to hold back his destructive power for a single minute longer, Velgor was unable to and quickly began to rip apart the Lambda and crush the two automatons as Sabé launched them into voidspace, coming out of hyperlight above an uninhabited astronomical object : taking Velgor to the astronomical object had been Sabé's intention as it would give the Shade Lord a place to practice and rediscover control over his powers. As the crippled Lambda crashed towards its surface, Velgor was thrown from the ship.
He was followed by Sabé, who donned a wing backpack and reached out. Both managed to grip the other's arm before tumbling into the sea, causing Velgor to lose his grip on Sabé. Reflecting on Draven Nul's declaration of power and suffering as he fell, Velgor decided he would continue inland on an island on his own, leaving Sabé unconscious on the shore. Velgor left a trail of footprints and destruction in his wake, through which Sabé followed him when she awoke. Velgor pushed the Handmaiden back when she approached and demanded he be left alone because of his out-of-control powers.
