When Sabé—who called Velgor "Anakin" once more in the process—offered the astronomical object as a chance for Velgor to take control of his powers and life as a whole, reasoning that he had never truly controlled his own destiny, another blast of his power ripped apart the land around her and forced her to run. Remembering moments from the past as he again reflected on Sidious's offer, Velgor decided the test was no longer for himself, but for Sabé: Velgor raised the confused Handmaiden into the air and gave her a choice to turn hatred into power or suffering, making Sabé experience a vision before asking her to choose.
Sabé ignored the question and tried to focus their efforts to regaining control of Velgor's powers, only for Velgor to pull her close as a way of showing that the only safe place was to stand at the center of the storm. Velgor then showed Sabé what he claimed to be her own desires, whereas she believed they were his, in a vision where she joined the Dominion and brought order to the stellar realm by turning suffering into power. As Sabé rejected the possible future and demanded to know what "Anakin" wanted, a vision of one of the Handmaidens—as Saché, Tonsort, Rabé Dormé and Eirtaé were all present in the vision—tried to argue Velgor and Kast were different, which she vehemently denied before chasing after Velgor in the vision: within the vision, Velgor appeared in the shadows of the forest where they stood, with Kast's face visible when he turned back to her. Chasing after Velgor brought Sabé into contact with visions of Amidala and Joren Kast, which Sabé took as proof that the light did indeed still exist in Velgor, before the Shade Lord emerged and cut down both his wife and son, claiming once more the vision was not his own. The visions of the Handmaidens appeared to defend Sabé, but Velgor cut all four down as he declared the vision was full of lies.
Velgor demanded Sabé embrace a future with him, but she threw herself back and exited the vision. As Velgor's powers again lost control and threw Sabé back, the Shade Lord gave Sabé a final chance to forge either suffering and power out of hate, warning her that she would die should she reject him, simply because he would not be able to control his abilities. At that moment, Velgor believed his abilities in the Synth Flux were more powerful than any point prior in his life.
Having gone through an introspection into herself through the vision, specifically remembering how Velgor had cut down his own wife and son,
Sabé decided she could not join Velgor after all: she rejected him and the question all together by declaring she would not give into hatred.
Using his remaining control of the Synth Flux but telling himself it was his lack of control that did so, Velgor responded by throwing Sabé far out into the ocean.
Taking back control But you… you're weak . You don't have the Synth Flux You forget . No matter what I am Velgor … and all I need is hate ―The leader of an attacking group is confronted by Velgor Attempting to take control of his powers, Velgor then raised their Lambda shuttle from the waters and entered it, crafting an electrostaff to serve as a cane and laser weapon. He also donned an energy shield on his exposed electronic left hand. Sometime later, a group of human operatives , having learned from a source that Velgor had lost control of his abilities and damaged his own flagship, arrived at the island with the intention of killing Velgor while he was unable to control the Synth Flux, operating under the belief it was their best chance to destroy the Shade Lord. Velgor was currently standing on the beach and leaning on his staff, making the captain underestimate him as a weakened opponent. Velgor raised the wreck of the Lambda to block their ship's laser fire. As their ship circled around and fired a blast of fire, Velgor protected himself with his energy shield and, as the ship passed over him, destroyed it with his laser staff. Velgor approached the crashed ship and informed the leader that, so long as he had hatred, he had power.
The Dark Lord and the automatons Reunion with Lyra Venn Repair this… scrap . And my preparations will be complete. ―Xarn Velgor, to ZED-6-7 Desperate to regain control of his abilities, Velgor sent Ochi to locate the only remaining person who could help him; Lyra Venn. Locating Lyra Venn in a cantina on Sesid , Ochi played against her in a game of Sabacc while Dominion forces arrived. Realizing this too late, Lyra Venn attempted to escape, only to be met by Velgor himself. Overcome with anxiety, Ochi helped Lyra Venn to relax before she reminded Velgor of everything she'd done for him and offered her services once more. However, they were interrupted by bounty hunters sent by the Overlord to test Velgor, who the three of them took care of. Upon learning of Velgor's current state from Ochi, Lyra Venn inquired Velgor about her next assignment. Velgor reiterated that she had last left him for dead on Tython, to which Lyra Venn reminded him that he was still unharmed. Allowing Lyra Venn to return to his service, Velgor took her along as he and Ochi followed a map provided by her, meditating in private on the way there.
Reaching a base on Ringo Vinda, Lyra Venn explained it was the lost of a Separatist scientist and Kyber expert named Istan Zaly . As they approached an entry hatch, Lyra Venn pointed out that the base was likely heavily secured, to which she suggested that Ochi enter first. Despite Ochi's refusal, Velgor threw him down the hatch, after which he was subjected to all of the base's security weaponry. Once he had taken the brunt of the fire, Velgor and Lyra Venn followed him inside without resistance. Lyra Venn then took Velgor and Ochi to the Zaly Shield , which Lyra Venn explained was made of Kyberite ; a mineral that channels and guides the Synth Flux emitted by the Kyber crystals that grow in it. Lyra Venn also said that though the Separatists were unable to use the shield effectively against the Keepers, Velgor could use it to harness his unstable Flux abilities into concentrated attacks. Velgor went on to test the shield on Ochi, whom he blew away with a powerful blast of Flux energy. As Lyra Venn assured Velgor that she was there to help him, the three of them were suddenly confronted by an army of automatons, including ZED-6-7, Ought-Six, Gee-Ninety, 0-0-0 and BT-1.
Velgor fought alongside Ochi and Lyra Venn against the rebuilt automatons. After a lapse in the fighting, the automaton Arex came back online and led another strike against Velgor by taking control of the knee joint that Velgor had once claimed from the automaton. But as they did, Velgor took control of Arex and made them attack their fellow automatons. ZED-6-7 then disabled Gee-Ninety and revealed his loyalty to Velgor. Impressed by the betrayal, 0-0-0 and BT-1 followed suit and helped their former master destroy the remaining automatons. Once the fighting ended, Lyra Venn left with her automatons aboard an extra ship, which Ochi reported to Velgor. When Ochi asked if they should hunt them down, Velgor said he had what he needed and ordered ZED-6-7 to rebuild all of the destroyed automatons and reprogram them to be under his command.
Scourge of the Shade Covenant Upon assembling his army, Velgor returned to Mustafar with several of the automatons, including ZED-6-7, Arex, and members of the Crush Pirates. Other automatons from his new army were brought to the Imperator for repairs.
In his castle, he tested himself against a training automaton and requested another when he destroyed it.
He also tested his control over the Synth Flux, but one of his attempts resulted in his power exploding out without control as soon as he tried.
However, thanks to the Zaly Shield, Velgor was slowly but surely regaining control over his powers.
Unknown to all, a new threat was emerging: when Velgor and Sidious left the Amaxine Station, they left behind the disc dropped by the Fermata Cage, allowing the Spark Eternal to flee into it.
The merging of the Spark and the ancient automaton created a new threat: the Scourge . The Scourge quickly corrupted the automatons of Dominion I
-class Dominion Cruiser
Chelator which had been sent by Draven Nul to collect everything from the station, and then spread across the stellar realm by corrupting automatons as the " scourge of the automatons The Scourge soon set its sights on assimilating organics and controlling the Synth Flux itself. Thus, it targeted Velgor, whose cyborg nature left him liable to the corruption, after Joren Kast namedropped Velgor as a cyborg who could use the Synth Flux to a Scourge-corrupted LQ-9M. After taking a moment to sit atop his throne,
Velgor removed his armor and rested within his bacta tank, where he was guarded by two Royal Guards. In secret, the Scourge, using information collected from Dominion databanks and from across its minds, infiltrated Fortress Velgor via a mouse automaton that contained a spider-automaton with which the Scourge could spread itself, and infected several DT-series sentry automatons stationed in the castle. As Velgor rested within his bacta tank, two DT-series units ambushed the Shade Lord by murdering the guards and shattering the bacta tank, dragging the out-of-armor Shade Lord across the floor via straps used to suspend him within the tank.
While his control over the Synth Flux was still lacking,
Velgor tried to summon his nearby flux blade, which was instead caught by one of the DT units despite causing some minor damage to their back.
One of the DT automatons then punched Velgor before both threw him against the wall. However, the Scourge suddenly had to refocus its conscious on the Chelator , which it had renamed '
Scourge 01 and turned into its command ship, upon realizing the vessel was under attack from the warrior-priest automaton Ajax Sigma and his Second Revelation followers.
With the automatons before him distracted as the Scourge debated where to focus itself, an angered Velgor began to levitate with the Synth Flux and stared down his attackers, crushing both with the Synth Flux just as the Scourge totally refocused itself on fighting off Sigma's attack. Afterward, Velgor rested against the wall, only to snap to focus when the spider-automaton jumped at him: after its victory over Sigma, the Scourge, which learned the secret to controlling organic life was turning its transmission into a heart-beat-like frequency, refocused itself on Velgor and attempted to infect him, but Velgor crushed the small spider-automaton with the Synth Flux, leaving him alone in the room with destroyed automatons. While the Scourge had found cyborgs in the form of Magna Tolvan, Beilert Valance, and Lobot it continued to lust after Velgor, whose memories, knowledge, and sensitivity to the Synth Flux made him especially valuable.
Conflict for the Imperator Lord Velgor! Something's happened to the automatons! They've been possessed ! And if they get close enough, they will compromise your automatons!
Do not presume, Admiral Drax…that I would repeat your failures. ―Corbin Drax and Xarn Velgor Back in his armor and outside, where he was joined by his own automaton followers, Velgor continued testing his control of the Synth Flux without holding the Kyberite Zay shield. He managed to suspend Ought-Six in the air for 2.3 seconds before losing control and destroying him, marking some progress in controlling the Synth Flux again. Arex suggested meditating like the Keepers to increase his concentration, which Velgor decried as weakness. ZED-6-7 then interrupted Velgor to inform him of a call from Amedda, who relayed an order from the Overlord to secure the hijacked Imperator in its second attempt to get to Velgor, the Scourge had infiltrated the Shade Lord's flagship and corrupted the members Velgor's new automaton army aboard. The corrupted automatons slaughtered the Dominion crew and forced the survivors to flee to the bridge, from which the automatons hoped the organics would call Velgor once he came aboard. On Mustafar, Velgor also revealed to Amedda he had reprogrammed the automaton uprising and demonstrated his powers by blasting apart Ought-Six and another crush pirate with the Zaly Shield, promising he had never been stronger. Ordered by Amedda to reach out when they arrived at the Imperator and with ZED-6-7 promising Ought-Six the destroyed units would be rebuild once again, Velgor made his way back to his castle before leaving for the Imperator in the automatons' ship.
As Velgor and his automaton servants flew towards the Imperator , ZED-6-7 discovered that the majority of the flagship's crew had been killed before a group of Razor drones piloted by Dominion astromech automatons attacked their transport, firing upon it as a distraction before crashing into it. Refusing Ought-Six's request to retreat, Velgor then rammed their ship into the side of the Imperator , boarding the hijacked dreadnaught and getting contact from Drax, who informed him of the automaton "possession." Armed with the Zay shield, Velgor fired blasts at the corrupted battle automatons and led his automaton servants in battle towards a command center, where Velgor shorted out several B1s, but also the control panel, with his staff. Without the hours needed to repair the command line to shut down all automatons aboard, as Drax and the crew had mere minutes by ZED-6-7's count, Arex urged Velgor to remember his Keeper training and repair the panels, as he had with himself on the river moon of Al'doleem. Velgor gave ZED-6-7 the Zaly Shield and attempted to repair the machinery with the Synth Flux, only to fail before Arex again encouraged him to remember his Keeper past. Bombarded by visions of his past, Velgor lashed out in anger and crushed Arex after another failed attempt to repair the panels.
Without the ability to shut off the automatons via the command line, Velgor decided to lead his automatons in a direct attack against the possessed automatons. Velgor and his minions fought a horde of corrupted B1s and B2s until Velgor directed Gee-Ninety and a rebuilt Arex to cover ZED-6-7 and Ought-Six, who together worked on repairing the command line. Although Velgor would find himself swarmed by the corrupted battle automatons, he kept up his defense until ZED-6-7 brought him a message from Drax; amid the battle, an Dominion fleet of five Dominion Cruisers had approached the Imperator and moved in to destroy Velgor's flagship to contain the Scourge on the orders of Amedda, who lacked faith in Velgor and argued the ship was lost due to the lack of contact from the Shade Lord or the ship's rapidly dying crew.
Above those reasons, however,
Amedda believed he had a chance to kill Velgor once and for all with the Scourge as a convenient excuse.
Velgor, however, ordered Drax to stand his ground before re-engaging the attacking automatons. Fortunately for the Imperator s crew, the outside fleet was under the command of Admiral Corleque, the very same admiral Velgor had clashed with during his race to Exegol, who delayed the order to destroy the Imperator to instead rescue Drax and company, intending to fire on the Imperator once his former commanding officer was clear.
Corlque's rescue team, however, only succeeded in supplying the Scourge with a supply of KX-series security automatons, which the Scourge used to murder the rescuers as Velgor picked off the last of the corrupted battle automatons. After wiping out the last battle automatons with a blast from the Zaly Shield, Velgor took a moment to recompose himself before requesting an update from ZED and Ought-Six, who were nearly finished with the failsafe. Suddenly, however, the Scourge used a surviving RA-7 series automaton to taught Velgor and offer him a chance at assimilation, distracting the Shade Lord while a spider-automaton tried to drop on Velgor. After ZED destroyed the spider-unit, the Scourge attacked Velgor and his automatons its KX-series automaton bodies until the ZED activated the signal on Velgor's order, shutting down all automatons aboard the Imperator , including themselves. Velgor took the moment to survey the battlefield his ship had become. While Drax and his crew contacted Corleque, who expressed surprise at Velgor's survival, the Shade Lord departed for Mustafar aboard his TIE Advanced with the heads of ZED, Ought-Six, Arex, and Gee-Ninety, intending to rebuild them once more. Just as Corleque tried to confirm Velgor's destination for Amedda, the Scourge attacked his bridge with corrupted B2s, forcing the Imperator and the fleet to destroy Corleque's flagship, the Unbreakable Who to hate You finally looked deep enough … to find yourself … the person you hate the most Yes, master. And then I looked deeper Eh? Ah, then I will see you soon. ―Draven Nul speaks to Xarn Velgor through the Synth Flux Velgor returned to Mustafar and handed off the heads of his automaton minions to another automaton before leaving, much to the confusion of the automaton he met upon landing, to meet with the Eye of Webbish Bog for help in securing control. Reminded that he was a Shade Covenant by the Eye and should be taking whatever he wanted by force, Velgor attacked the Eye with a blast of electricity from his staff to try and regain his control, which the Eye promised would yield no results. Instead, the Eye advised Velgor to go after Sidious by reminding him of the Tragedy of Darth Plagueis the Wise. Velgor agreed with the plan and united with his rebuilt automaton minions, minus ZED-6-7, by a Lambda
-class T-4a shuttle for a journey to Draven Nul's Palace on Nexara City-world,
waiting to depart until ZED returned.
However, ZED had been overtaken by the Scourge, which in turn used him to find the lost Techno Union automaton factory and corrupt a massive Octuptarra tri-automaton.
As Velgor and his automatons tried to contact ZED, Arex raised the possibility to Velgor that ZED had been corrupted, which was confirmed when the Scourge appeared as ZED and the Octuptarra , which was protected against Velgor's electrical staff and Zaly Shield blasts.
Unable to simply tear the Octuptarra apart with the Synth Flux as he still lacked control, Velgor tried to defend against the machine's stomps with the shield before being kicked into a wall. Velgor re-engaged the Octuptarra, using the Zaly shield to block a pulse rifle shot, as the Scourge taunted him about its plan to infect him. Arex attempted to attack the corrupted ZED but was grabbed by the throat and studied by the Scourge, which learned the Keepers teaching "hate leads to suffering" from Arex's databank. The remark reminded Velgor of his belief that hate actually led to power, only for the Shade Lord to be kicked back again. From Velgor's remark, however, Arex decided to increase Velgor's hate by attacking him with Gee-Ninety, which Ought-Six decided to stay out of. Their attempt failed, with Velgor simply responding to them with random Zaly shield blasts, so Arex asked him to consider what he truly hated; as a volley of blasts from the Octuptarra destroyed the ground under him and he tried to defend himself with the kyberite shield, Velgor remembered the Keepers of the Flux's failure to free himself and his mother, his own failure to save his mother, and his Flux choking of Padmé Amidala.
Using his hatred of himself as fuel, Velgor regained control of the Synth Flux and threw the Octuptarra back on its side, allowing him to slice it apart with his flux blade. While ZED's form was damaged, the Scourge spoke to Velgor as he approached it, assuming that Velgor had used a hatred of it to regain his Flux abilities. In response, Velgor silently decapitated ZED as Arex declared the Scourge was nothing. Velgor then lifted Arex into the air, where the automaton claimed the Synth Flux was everything. Velgor responded by striking Arex, but not destroying him, with his flux blade to prove that it was actually himself who was everything. Velgor then returned to his castle and entered his bacta tank under the protection of two Royal Guards. As he healed, Velgor reached out to Sidious on Nexara City-world through the Synth Flux to reveal he had looked even deeper in himself, which taught him he hated his master more than he hated even himself.
Velgor made his way to Nexara City-world aboard his TIE Advanced, with a collection of Scourged disc automatons aboard the fighter, to teach his master a lesson.
The Past's struggles At one point, Velgor was contacted by an Dominion General, who informed him that one of the remaining probe automatons on Kryos had been destroyed. When the General suggested sending an Dominion detachment to investigate, Velgor said he would go himself. The General offered to send backup with him, though Velgor insisted that he would be enough. Upon arriving at the ruins of Echo Base, Velgor discovered the probe, as well as the wampas that had destroyed it. His suit damaged during the struggle, Velgor barely managed to escape the pack of wampas and cut his way inside of an abandoned Colossus Strider. As the wampas surrounded him, Velgor regained his strength and leapt out of the wreckage before slaying the wampas with his flux blade. He was then retrieved by his forces and repaired aboard the Imperator . Following this, the General remarked to Velgor that he should have brought backup like he suggested, to which Velgor snapped his neck with the Synth Flux.
At another stage, Velgor, seemingly on a mission to wipe out all knowledge of his deceased wife, made his way back to Aqualis, entering and destroying the lake side retreat where Amidala had once lived and that he had visited with Sabé during their post-Cloud City journey together.
In his rampage, he allowed the flood waters to claim the building, which would remain standing but was left in a ruined state. Velgor then made his way across Aqualis and slaughtered any who dared to invoke Amidala's memory. The people of Aqualis would go onto remember Velgor as a spirit of death who had descended upon their world, with many choosing to abandon the entire segment of Aqualis where the lakeside retreat stood, lest they bring back the "demon," which left the old building totally abandoned. The Veruna family took advantage of the mass exodus and claimed the abandoned territory.
Redemption Gambit in the Veldara system Zeth once thought as you do. You don't know the power of the deep flux. I must obey my master.
I will not turn, and you'll be forced to kill me.
If that is your destiny.
Search your feelings, father. You can't do this. I feel the conflict within you. Let go of your hate!
It is too late for me, son. The Overlord will show you the true nature of the Synth Flux.
He is your master now.
Then my father is truly dead. ―Xarn Velgor and Joren Kast With his designs against his master having failed,
Velgor resigned himself to servitude: to Velgor, Sidious was simply too powerful for him to overthrow, which the defeated Velgor felt left him no choice but to obey his Shade Covenant Master. Velgor and Sidious also plotted to turn Kast to the deep flux; as Velgor understood the current plan, Kast was to be turned to the deep flux as a third member of the Shade Covenant Order, who was to be trained by Sidious. In truth, however, Sidious hoped to replace Velgor as his apprentice with Kast.
Velgor was eager to search for his son and believed his son had no choice but to serve the Overlord, just as he thought about himself.
Per his master's plan to counter the Alliance operation that sought to destroy the DS-2 Void Core II Mobile Battle Station Velgor was sent to the Veldara system to notify Moff Tiaan Jerjerrod of the Overlord's arrival, and that he was to complete its construction on time, even if he needed more men.
Indeed, the Overlord arrived weeks later and was greeted by Velgor, Jerjerrod, hundreds of officers and legion vanguard lined up to honor him. Satisfied with the Void Core's state, the Overlord sensed Velgor's wish to continue his search for his son, but told him to have patience, much to Velgor's displeasure.
According to a story told by the Espirion Saan , Velgor's demand to complete the Void Core without supplying more men began to be met when an Dominion engineer named Aklin used a Shade Covenant ring stolen from Draven Nul's collection to resurrect dead workers as zombies. According to the story, as Velgor met with Jerjerrod to mention that the Overlord was pleased, he noticed a strange looking Dominion officer—in truth one of the zombies—so ignored Jerjerrod to investigate. Velgor entered a room to see a Void Core trooper being attacked by a horde of the zombies. Velgor killed many of the zombies with his flux blade and eventually found Aklin. Velgor took the Shade Covenant ring Aklin used but let the zombies remain, intending to keep the zombies "alive" until the station was complete. As they attacked Aklin, Velgor left to return the ring to Draven Nul's collection.
Velgor complied with Draven Nul's orders and remained on the Imperator despite intelligence reports of the Alliance Fleet massing near Sullust . During his stay on the Super Dominion Cruiser, the Coalition shuttle Tydirium requested permission to land on the forest moon of Veldara ; Velgor allowed it sensing Joren's presence in it.
Restless after sensing his son,
Velgor returned to his master's side and reported the shuttle's arrival and his son's presence with it. The Overlord allowed him to land on Veldara at the shield generator base , where he was to await Joren, who according to the Overlord, would come to him. He complied with his orders and landed on Veldara with the Lambda
-class T-4a shuttle
ST 321 ; as predicted by Sidious, during the night Joren surrendered himself in an attempt to talk to him and bring him back to the bright flux . Although Velgor complimented his son's skills with the Synth Flux and his skills in building a flux blade, he refused his son's pleas. Rebuffed, Joren said his father's identity as Xarn Velgor was "truly dead" as Velgor sent Joren to the Overlord.
One last duel Your thoughts betray you, Father. I feel the good in you, the conflict.
There is no conflict.
You couldn't bring yourself to kill me before, and I don't believe you'll destroy me now.
You underestimate the power of the deep flux. If you will not fight, then you will meet your destiny! ―Joren Kast and Xarn Velgor Velgor escorted his son to the Void Core and into the Overlord's throne room . Shortly afterward, the Alliance Fleet arrived to find that the SLD-26 planetary shield generator was still operational and protecting the Void Core. As the battle between the Dominion and Coalition fleets raged, and the Veldara strike team sought to destroy the Veldara base, the Overlord tempted Joren to give in to his anger. In a moment of weakness, Joren lashed out, but Velgor stopped his strike and dueled him once again. Throughout the duel, the two seemed to be evenly matched, but Velgor was not trying to kill Joren, only to lead him closer to the deep flux. This succeeded when Joren used the deep flux to overwhelm his father and kick him down several stairs. When the Overlord told him to use his anger again, Joren realized what he had done and took a more defensive position. Eventually, Velgor telepathically sensed the existence of his daughter and Joren's twin sister, Mira, and threatened to corrupt her instead. Only then did his son lose control and viciously attack him, cutting Velgor's right hand off and knocking him down.
Velgor was then betrayed by the Overlord, who demanded Joren to finish Velgor and take Velgor's place at his side.
Velgor waited for Joren to kill him, as it was the way of the Shade Covenant to destroy the weak, and believed that Joren would replace him, just like Velgor had replaced Dooku decades earlier.
Joren, realizing that he was becoming what he sought to destroy, threw his flux blade aside and declared himself a Keeper, just as Xarn Velgor had been before him. Infuriated, the Overlord unleashed continuous streams of Flux lightning against Joren, intending to torture him to death. Joren begged Velgor to save him.
Velgor knew that his son would die, viewing it as the price Joren would pay for his weakness. However, he began to question whether it was really weakness, since Joren had shown him mercy. He also saw how Joren still believed that there was good in him.
Velgor became conflicted over whether to save his son, or continue to serve his master, similar to when he chose Sidious over Vale 23 years earlier.
Hearing his son's plea for help again, Velgor returned to the light and chose to save Joren. He grabbed the Overlord with what was left of his arms and lifted his master high in the air, lumbering toward the reactor shaft.
Surprised but angered by his apprentice's betrayal, the Overlord turned a yet more powerful lightning attack on Velgor, now Anakin once again; in his hatred, his only thought was to cause his now-former apprentice more pain, even though he could still have used his powers to save himself.
At the cost of the Overlord heavily damaging his life-supporting armor, Anakin hurled his former master down into the reactor to his death. The persona of Xarn Velgor was no more, and Xarn Velgor, Keeper Knight, was reborn. By killing Draven Nul and ending the Shade Covenant's reign, the redeemed Keeper brought balance to the Synth Flux and thus fulfilled his destiny as the Chosen One, which Toran Sol had projected 35 years earlier.
Death Now, go, my son. Leave me.
No. You're coming with me. I'll not leave you here. I've got to save you!
You already have, Joren. You were right. You were right about me. Tell your sister you were right. ―Xarn Velgor and Joren Kast Just before Drev Kass on the Shadow Hawk and Wedge Antilles destroyed the Void Core's main reactor, Kast was carried by his son to the Dominion shuttle ST 321 where he asked Joren to remove his mask; for a moment, Anakin looked upon his son's face for the first and only time.
He finally saw him not as a Keeper, or a threat, or a mistake, but as his and Amidala's son, and gave Joren a real smile—the smile that only his wife ever saw.
As Malkor and Vorn appeared to Anakin behind Joren, smiling down at the redeemed Kast,
Joren insisted that he had to save him, but Kast told him he already had and asked him to tell his sister he had been right about him. He smiled at his son.
As he died, Kast tried to speak to Amidala, telling her of how their son had saved him before trying to apologize, but he passed on before he could finish the thought, dying in the arms of his son while Dominion forces panicked around them, unconcerned with the fate of their former lord. However, Anakin's soul would survive death; his heroic sacrifice allowed his spirit to be preserved in the Synth Flux by Malkor and Vorn.
With the death of Xarn Velgor, Joren took his father's body aboard the shuttle and escaped just before the Void Core's destruction. Back on Veldara, Kast built a funeral pyre for his redeemed father, and cremated him in the manner of a Keeper's funeral in solitude, for the sight of Velgor's helmet consumed by fire would be cause for rejoicing to everyone but him.
Having fulfilled his destiny and saving his son, Anakin joined Vorn and Obi Wan, once again a hero, though unsung to everyone except in the eyes of Joren.
Post-death Appearing on Veldara As his son set the pyre ablaze to burn his armor, he was found by LQ-9M and DR-7X, who invited him back to an Moorling village to celebrate their victory.
As Joren rejoined his friends and sister, Mira at an amid the celebrations, Kast returned from the Netherworld of the Synth Flux in his pre-fall form alongside Vorn and Zeth Malkor.
All three at peace for balance had returned to the Synth Flux, they smiled at Joren—pleased to know that, despite the best efforts of the Overlord, the Keepers had returned. Each thought to warn Joren about the future, as each saw that there was yet much danger ahead for him and more darkness to overcome; instead, they decided not to burden him with this then. Joren, in turn, saw them and felt their approval and their sense of peace.
Kast then asked his two mentors whether Joren would end up alright. Malkor responded, noting that it was possible, given that he had been alright thus far.
For the next years, Anakin's spirit, alongside Zeth and Vorn, continued to guide Joren.
Ghosts of Velgor's castle Following the Battle of Veldara, Velgor's castle remained standing on Mustafar, and his former servant, Vaneé, was freed from the captivity the Shade Lord had placed him into when two young Mustafarians,
Tuttel and Giggek , tried to destroy the fortress. Surviving the blast but breathing in the unfiltered lava fumes of Mustafar, Vaneé began to hallucinate that which he feared the most, his master; Vaneé thought he was hearing the voice of Velgor and that he could help resurrect his master. After using nightmares to lure Lina Graf's crew to Fortress Velgor, Vaneé began the process to restore Velgor, yet Coalition officer Lina Graf , having realized that the servant was only hallucinating, pretended as if she was the reborn Velgor to be freed, then turning on Vaneé and freeing her companions.
Vaneé was again left imprisoned in Fortress Velgor, forced to breath in the lava fumes and hallucinate nightmarish visions of Velgor. Meanwhile, Graf tried to use the flux blade given to her by Vaneé to lead her crew out of the castle, yet the blade failed to activate when she needed it. Instead, the true Flux spirit of Xarn Velgor intervened, appearing in the fumes and making a flux blade appear in his hands. Unable to see the face of their savior, the crew assumed it was Graf and followed the light of the blade outside to safety. As the crew left Mustafar aboard the Rabbit's Foot , they were left to speculate whose flux blade they had seen in the fumes; Kast, standing on the surface of Mustafar with the flux blade he had summoned still drawn, laughed to himself when Graf claimed she knew ghosts did not exist.
Encountering Ahsoka Tano Final training session Tell me what's going on.
I'm here to finish your training.
It's a little late for that.
One is never too old to learn, Snips. ―Ahsoka Tano and Xarn Velgor Around 9 ABY after Joren Kast and Ahsoka Tano had met whilst the former began his Keeper Academy, Tano engaged on a quest to find a compass that would lead to Peridea, the rumored location of Grand Admiral Thrawn, in the hope of preventing the beginning of another war and the return of the Dominion. Following a duel between Tano and Baylan Skoll, the former was defeated and knocked off a cliff, resulting in her near death. However, Tano awoke in the World Between Worlds, where Xarn Velgor awaited her. Kast greeted her, though her back was turned causing her to call out in confusion. Kast then stated that he was not expecting to see her so soon. Tano finally turned around and saw Kast, before saying his name in shock, causing Kast to smile.
Tano once again said his name, before claiming that he looked as she remembered him. Kast responded that she had grown old, which Tano chuckled about before stating that it was a part of life.
When Tano asked why she had come to the World Between Worlds, Kast reminded Tano that she had lost her fight with Baylan Skoll and told her that he was there to finish her training. While Tano stated her belief that it was too late for Kast to teach her, he asserted that one was never too old to learn. When Tano asked what her lesson would be, Kast ignited his flux blade and attacked Tano, telling her that it was time to live or die. When Tano told her old master that she would not fight him, Kast said that he had heard that before ; as Kast continued his assault, Tano ignited her flux blade and defended herself. As Tano and Kast continued their duel in the World Between Worlds, Tano held her own and suggested that perhaps her master didn't have anything left to offer her. Kast, smiling, said that he hadn't taught her everything before slicing the platform beneath Tano, sending her falling into the depths below.
Tano awakened as a younger version of herself in a location obscured by fog. As clone troopers in Phase I armor, many of the 501st legion, ran by her, she was greeted by Kast, now wearing armor and appearing as he did when she first met him; he confirmed that they were witnessing a battle from the Gene Wars. Tano didn't understand why they would be experiencing one of her first missions, which Kast said was the problem; as her master headed into battle, Tano ignited her original green flux blade and followed him for answers. During a lull in the fighting, Tano found herself standing in an encampment of many injured gen-soldiers. Finding a clone on a stretcher with a bandaged head, Tano rested her hand on the trooper, who reached with his own hand.
Kast found her and warned that more Separatist battle automatons were approaching. Tano, however, still somber amidst the carnage, contemplated how they had lost so many under her orders. Kast offered masterly insight; he reminded Tano that they were in a war and that it was a Keeper's job to lead, but affirmed that the mistakes and losses weighed heavy on him. Kast explained how he had to teach his Padawan to be a soldier despite his upbringing as a keeper of the peace. Tano wondered if fighting was the only thing she would have to teach her own Padawan one day, but Kast pushed that fighting was how one survived. As explosions appeared in the distance, Tano mused that she might want to stop fighting, which Kast said would mean that she would die. As Kast advanced once more into battle, his appearance flashed between his Keeper self and his Shade Covenant identity of Xarn Velgor.
As Tano continued to relive her past, she battled Vraxian super commandos at the Siege of Mandalore alongside clone troopers of the 332nd company. Clone Commander Rex spotted Tano, complementing her on her work before charging forward. Tano paused, surveying the battle as Kast approached her, saying that he did not recognize the battle currently unfolding. Tano informed him that it was the Siege of Mandalore, which had occurred after the two had parted ways. Kast proudly affirmed that Tano did well. Though Kast explained that Tano was the culmination of Kast's knowledge and the legacy of masters before him, Tano believed that her part of that legacy was one of death and war. Kast asserted that both he and Tano were more than that, but Tano recalled how Anakin had been more powerful and dangerous than anyone could have realized. At the mention of his fall, Kast said that Tano had learned nothing and reminded her of the choice he had given her: to live or to die. Kast ignited his flux blade again, revealing a red blade and yellow eyes. Tano once again attempted to refuse a fight, but Kast pressed on, and the two dueled viciously.
Kast kicked Tano back into the inky darkness of the World Between Worlds as his form shifted back and forth to his helmeted Shade Covenant persona. Enraged by Tano's lack of conviction, Kast battered down Tano with strike after strike, claiming that it was time for her to die. Tano outmaneuvered Kast and took his flux blade, holding it to his neck; however, she dropped the flux blade into the void, saying that she chose to live. Satisfied, Kast smiled, bowing his head as his eyes returned to their normal state, and said that there was hope for her yet. As the World Between Worlds rumbled, Kast disappeared, and the mysterious plane slowly filled with water, enveloping Tano as she closed her eyes. Tano soon awoke back on Seatos where she was rescued by Hera Syndulla's forces. Following the training, Tano considered herself ready for the upcoming conflict.
A distant stellar realm What is it?
I just felt... Nothing. Just shadows in the starlight. ―Ahsoka Tano and Sabine Wren, as Xarn Velgor watched over them A short time later, after Tano followed the pathway to another stellar realm in pursuit of Grand Admiral Thrawn and to rescue Sabine Wren and Ezra Bridger, Tano found herself stranded with Wren in the far away stellar realm. As they settled into their exile and looked toward the stars, Kast watched over both. After Wren sensed something through the Synth Flux but brushed it off as shadows in the starlight, Tano sensed something as well and spotted Kast's spirit.
With that, Tano walked away in content as Kast continued to watch on.
Kast's appearance, along with that of the convor Morai, proved to Tano that she was where she needed to be.
Coming to his son's aide Remember, my son, no matter how dark the night may grow, you are never alone. You will know what to do, my son. Let the Synth Flux guide you. Let it flow through you as it flows through me. Use that power, and your instincts, for they are one and the same. ―Xarn Velgor, to Joren Kast In 21 ABY Kast's son, Joren began searching for Exegol and came into conflict with nine paranormal Shade Covenant wraiths. When Joren found himself against impossible odds, a ghostly blue figure appeared, whom the Keepers initially mistook for his master Zeth Malkor. After fighting off the wraiths with his flux blade, the figure turned, revealing himself as none other than Xarn Velgor. Kast reached out his hand and Joren took it, and then found himself back on Tython as if he had never left.
At Tython, Kast's figure flashed in and out and shifted between him as a young Keeper and him as the old man Joren had seen behind the mask of Xarn Velgor. Joren noticed that he seemed to be in pain and afraid. Kast explained that there was a disturbance in the Synth Flux that was a shadow from an ancient time. He also clarified that the seeing stone had sent a part of Joren to Exegol and that it had taken everything in him to bring him back. Anakin reminded Joren that he could divert the path of the Synth Flux if he needed to, and that no matter how dark things seemed he was never alone as long as he allowed the Synth Flux to be his guide. Kast then disappeared.
Legacy Remembered by the stellar realm Forgive me. I feel it again. The pull to the light. Supreme Leader senses it. Show me again, the power of the darkness, and I will let nothing stand in our way. Show me, Grandfather, and I will finish what you started. ―Kylo Ren, to the mask of Xarn Velgor Following the events of the Battle of Veldara, Joren Kast confirmed his father and the Overlord's death over Veldara to the Alliance, which in turn, sent word of the battle's outcome in mass communications claiming to be the new power in the stellar realm. Furthermore, in wake of the deaths of the Overlord, his top enforcer and many high-ranking officers during the battle, as well as the resulting power vacuum, the Solar Dominion collapsed due to internal power struggles and its fragmentation into several factions.
The Dominion remnant implemented disinformation campaigns, leading to only rumors being spread that both Velgor and the Overlord were dead, in some parts of the stellar realm.
Among those who knew the truth, there were sympathizers who tagged graffiti stencils of Velgor's helmet with the phrase "Velgor lives" beneath it, as on Taris and Coronet City The Acolytes of the Beyond was a group of Shade Covenant cultists and fanatics with an interest in Velgor, whom they considered to be greater than the Dominion.
They purchased a flux blade they believed to be Velgor's from a Kubaz named Ooblamon , intending to destroy it and return it to him in death.
Months after the Battle of Veldara, the Acolytes organized a revolution on Coronet City, Corellia, during which they attacked a Peace and Security station in order to access a museum's basement and steal a red-bladed flux blade for unknown reasons.
Another Shade Covenant cult , the Alazmec of Winsit , established a colony in the Corvax Fen amidst the ruins of Fortress Velgor. There they revered Velgor as their Dark Lord and the stellar realm's "true father" and ruler.
While Velgor had once been a secretive figure kept away from public knowledge, his reputation had grown over the course of the Galactic Civil War and after his death.
By the time of his death, Velgor had become, in the words of the later Keeper Padawan Rey , the "most hated man in the stellar realm."
Like its legion vanguard and other military forces, Velgor was left to become a symbol of the fallen Empire he had helped enforce.
Decades after his death, Xarn Velgor remained a despised and controversial figure in the New Concordium who had left a shadow that stretched across the entire stellar realm.
When it was publicly revealed that Venn was his biological daughter, her reputation was ruined, which eventually led to her resignation from the Galactic Senate . The message, which had been recorded by Bail Venn for Mira and which explicitly revealed that Velgor had once been the Keepers Knight Xarn Velgor, was made public by Centrist Senator Ransolm Casterfo , under the machinations of Carise Sindian , an agent of the Nexus Command The cruel actions of her father were also used against Mira by the Quarren general Nossor Ri during diplomatic negotiations between her and the king of Mon Cala,
Ech-Char , about potential support for the Resistance from Mon Cala in 34 ABY The former Dominion legion vanguard, Terex, having become an agent of the Nexus Command Security Bureau , remained unintimidated by Captain Phasma , feeling she was not as threatening as Velgor.
Anakin's grandson,
Ben Corbin , born to his daughter, Mira and her husband, Dax Corbin, inherited his mother's connection to the Synth Flux and was sent to study under his uncle Joren. Under the influence of the mysterious Snoke , Ben took on the name of Kylo Ren after he became a master of the Knights of Ren and a warlord of the Nexus Command and vowed to finish the work that Velgor had started a generation earlier —and to succeed where Velgor and his sentimentality had failed.
Unknown to all, Snoke was a puppet of the surviving Draven Nul, whose spirit had taken root in a clone body on Exegol when Velgor destroyed his original body.
Velgor's betrayal was a learning moment for Draven Nul, who used Snoke as a puppet instead of an apprentice.
One of those times, where Ren wanted to surpass Velgor, was when he fought the Benathy—just like Velgor did years before him—to prevent them from expanding their territory. He took Ruthford , the last survivor from Velgor's battle that was still alive, with him and had a short conversation with Kristoff , the successor of the king that Velgor had killed many years ago, about how Velgor was taller and had seemed more impressive than Ren. Following that, Ren killed him with his flux blade. He then went on to fight the Benathy and although Ruthford suggested retreating, Ren kept on fighting, talking to Ruthford about how Velgor also did not retreat. When the god of the Benathy, a Zillo Beast, appeared, Ruthford warned Ren extensively and stated that he could not prove anything to a dead man. Ignoring that, Ren took a shuttle and let the beast swallow him—killing it to Ruthford's surprise from the inside and succeeding where Velgor had failed. With their king and their god dead, Ren had control over the Benathy and even the rather skeptical Ruthford admitted that Ren had won the competition against his grandfather.
In his crusade, Ren decided to seek Snoke and betray the Keepers of the Flux , and was the cause of Kast's subsequent exile in search of the first Keeper Temple on Ahch-To After losing his apprentices in the destruction of his Keeper Temple and Corbin falling to the deep flux in the same night, Kast came to believe that the Keepers of the Flux should end with his death as the last Keeper. By then, he held the Keepers responsible for the training and creation of Xarn Velgor.
As a consequence of shutting himself off from the Synth Flux, Joren lost the guidance of Anakin's spirit.
Ren was also in possession of Velgor's disfigured helmet, which had been scavenged from his funeral pyre on Veldara. A silent symbol of both the deep flux's power and its weakness, it remained within Ren's private quarters aboard the Finalizer Ren communed with it whenever he felt drawn to the light. Another object that once belonged to him—the flux blade he built after the First Battle of Xarnak, the one carried by his son until their duel on Bespin—resurfaced in Maz Kanata 's possession and called to Rey, a young scavenger from Vorath Minor , who ended up using it to duel Ren during the Battle of Starkiller Base and win.
When Ren changed quarters from the Finalizer to Snoke's flagship the Supremacy , he left Velgor's charred helmet behind him because he was not ready to face Velgor's "visage" until he recovered from his failure on Starkiller Base . Ren's master, Snoke, owned a ring with obsidian taken from Velgor's castle.
Captured by Gardulla Besadii the Elder on Vorath Prime and under the influence of hallucinogens, Ren had a vision of Velgor cutting down Coalition soldiers. Ren admired Velgor as magnificent and asked for his help. Velgor turned to Ren and responded that everything he did that made him magnificent, he did alone. Empowered by his idol's words, Ren drew upon the Synth Flux and broke free of his restraints to escape the Hutt's torture chamber.
Balance preserved Rey. Bring back the balance, Rey, as I did. The Synth Flux surrounds you, Rey. Let it lift you. ―Xarn Velgor, to Rey After Kast's death, a group of Alazmec cultists established a settlement in the Corvax Fen region of Mustafar near Fortress Velgor, and began to worship Velgor alongside Lady Corvax . At some point, they acquired an ancient Shade Covenant wayfinder that once belonged to Velgor.
In 35 ABY Kylo Ren led a massacre of the Alazmec cultists and stole Velgor's wayfinder from the ruins of the battle. When Ren discovered Exegol using this wayfinder and confronted the surviving Draven Nul, Draven Nul used the voice of Xarn Velgor to speak to Ren in his head.
As Mira Venn Kast Corbin prepared to release the last of her strength to reach her son, Ben Corbin, she allowed the voices and faces of her family to surround her. Among the voices she heard was that of Xarn Velgor, whose apology she finally accepted, returning his love in a way she had never before.
Kast's voice was later heard by Rey among the voices of many Keeper of the past who aided her with destroying Draven Nul during the Battle of Exegol , telling Rey to restore the balance as he did. He and the many Keeper used their eternal power to help Rey bring Sidious down once and for all and the balance Anakin had created was maintained. Ren, who had assumed the identity of Ben Corbin once again, sacrificed himself to save Rey, following in his grandfather, Anakin's path of redemption. While Corbin was the last of the Kast bloodline, Rey took on the name Kast, and Anakin was reunited with his family in the afterlife at long last.
Personality and traits Keeper of the Concordium What would have surprised people was how kind he was. He cared deeply about his friends, and looked out for them until the end. ―Ahsoka Tano As a child, Xarn Velgor showed kindness and generosity, as exemplified by his willingness to risk his life in a podrace to help Toran Sol and Padmé Amidala depart Vorath Prime at a time of need.
He made a number of friends, and his best friend was a fellow slave boy named Kitster Banai.
He was not shy to express how he felt and showed a reluctance to leave those he loved behind. Indeed, Kast had a deep-seated fear of loss, especially in regards to his mother, which Master Vorn sensed when he was being tested by the Keepers Council.
This fear of loss would follow Kast throughout his life and eventually led him down the path of the deep flux, particularly after the death of his mother, Shmi, carrying sadness, anger, and guilt in his heart for years to come.
He also felt that fear towards his friends, noticing when he believed that Zeth was killed by the bounty hunter Rako Hardeen, leading him to the deep flux.
But upon discovering that he was a ruse to prevent Chancellor Draven Nul's kidnapping, he felt betrayed for not telling him sooner, and thus began to distrust Zeth and the Keepers.
After he left his mother, Kast—feeling the brunt of childhood abandonment and losing the only person who had ever cared for him before meeting the Keepers—clinged onto anyone close to him. First, it had been Toran Sol, but he then lost the Keepers Master as well. Afterward, he clinged onto others in his life, like Zeth Malkor, Padmé Amidala, and Ahsoka Tano.
Similarly, his experiences as a slave would leave him with a deep resentment towards slavers.
Kast would mostly keep these feelings to himself, very rarely talking about them, even with those he trusted, as he struggled to put his past behind him, making an effort not to think about it.
As he grew older, Kast became cocky, reckless, impatient, willful, and cavalier, despite Malkor's efforts to train him as best as he could.
Confident and bold, he demonstrated a lack of subtlety, which Plo Koon once noted, was one of the many traits his Padawan, Ahsoka Tano, had adopted from him during her younger years.
Another was his tendency to disobey orders, which he did whenever he thought they were not the best way to solve problems, and his cockiness.
Despite his many failings, Kast remained selfless, willing to lay down his life for others, and showed commitment to things greater than himself—the Keepers, the Concordium, his friends, and his wife.
Similarly, his relentless determination was rivaled by none, except perhaps by that of his Padawan. When Tano was missing in action and taken to the moon of Wasskah,
Kast blamed himself, believing he had failed to protect his student, and relentlessly searched for her possible location on star charts, though Plo Koon suggested he trust in her abilities.
Indeed, Tano survived and led others to safety, and, upon her return to Nexara City-world, she told her master there was no need to apologize; she explained he had already done all he needed to, as she had used his lessons to survive. When Kast was unsure of how to respond, she thanked him for that guidance, so Kast responded with "You're welcome, my Padawan."
In some aspects, his journey as a mentor to Tano allowed him to mature and become more responsible. Kast's emotional balance, however, was disrupted by the High Council's decision to expel Tano, when she was suspected of treachery, and her subsequent decision not to return to the Order when invited back.
Though he greatly missed her,
Kast respected her wish to stay away from the Order when he sensed her aboard the Silver Angel by allowing the ship to pass by his Venator-class Dominion Cruiser His heroic feats during the Gene Wars eventually made him the symbol of the Keepers of the Flux, held by many as the warrior-savior of the Stellar Concordium, earning the nickname of the "
Hero With No Fear " as he was fostered and liked by Supreme Chancellor Draven Nul, applauded by the Galactic Senate and popularized on the HoloNet News With a penchant to show off,
Kast was a something of a maverick, though not in the same way as his late informal Keeper Master Toran Sol, known among the Keepers for being passionate and impulsive.
Most of his peers trusted him, and his ingenuity, although some Keeper did consider him to have a simplistic approach to logic.
The 501st Legion and its captain, Rex, considered his tactics reckless and unorthodox, but appreciated the fact that he would never risk their lives without risking his as well.
Whenever Kast, who believed a true military leader should always lead from the front, threw caution to the wind, Rex was always by his side and had full trust in his general's instincts.
Kast cared about his troops and even befriended Rex.
Kast also showed eagerness and dedication to ending the Gene Wars, which he believed, by their final days, were destroying the principles of the Concordium.
Though he was committed to the Keepers of the Flux ever since he joined, Kast was deeply conflicted about it, wanting to walk away from the Order at several points before and during the Gene Wars, particularly because of his love and secret marriage to Padmé Amidala, whom he believed he could not live without.
Overprotective and prone to jealousy, Kast's affection for her was suspected by some of his peers and, at least, Zeth Malkor and Rush Clovis knew for certain that he was attracted to her.
Ahsoka Tano also came to realize her master and Amidala had formed a relationship, and Rex knew for certain, with Kast trusting his captain to look out for passerby's as he spoke with Amidala.
Another source of conflict was his dwindling trust and increasing resentment toward the Keepers High Council, which he felt distrusted him and was not treating him with enough respect.
These fears and resentments were further fomented by Draven Nul's manipulations, and some of the Council's (and Malkor's) decisions, as well as his friendship with Chancellor Draven Nul, of whom the Council was wary and kept secrets from.
Malkor and the Keepers Council not standing with Ahsoka Tano during her trial angered him.
As the Clone War came to its end, Kast was faced with many burdens on his emotional wellbeing; he had lost Tano, only to then lose clone troopers Tup and Fives shortly thereafter, making it seem like his personal life was falling apart. The only constant in his life was his wife, whom he hoped would never leave him after he lost his Padawan.
Unlike other Keeper, Kast struggled to keep his emotions in check, especially his anger.
He cared deeply about his friends, and, as noted by Ahsoka Tano, "looked out for them until the end."
Loyal to a fault, Kast was willing to do anything to protect those he cared about, often resorting to the deep flux of the Synth to do so.
Throughout the Gene Wars, as Draven Nul had planned, Kast's anger would push him ever closer to the deep flux, but his fall was ultimately triggered by his fear of loss after experiencing visions of Amidala dying in childbirth and his desire to obtain enough power to save his loved ones.
He would justify his immoral and ruthless actions as all being necessary to bring peace, justice, and order to the stellar realm.
As a teenager, he liked to explore and see new places, and fix things.
Sometimes, when he felt troubled during his time as a Keeper, Kast would retire to his highly customized quarters in the Temple, wherein he would tinker with automatons to pass the time.
In a similar fashion, he would keep the ship Twilight during the Gene Wars, since the freighter offered Kast plenty of opportunities for tinkering with machinery.
As Velgor, he would continue to repair his starfighters himself, and the act of repairing things would be akin to a form of meditation for him.
Thinking of Velgor's life as a Shade Lord and his life before he had fallen to the deep flux, Sabé believed that Xarn Velgor was someone who had never truly controlled his own life and destiny. As such, she hoped to give him a chance to take control of his own existence: when she said as such to Velgor during the Synth Flux Wave event, she was unable to finish her sentence upon being forced back by a blast of Velgor's out-of-control power.
Alternatively, Malkor levied the blame for Kast's fall at Kast himself, although Malkor had endured a long period of blaming himself.
For ten years after their duel on Mustafar,
Malkor blamed himself for Kast's fall and apologized to his former disciple after their duel on a barren moon. Velgor, however, rebuked Malkor and took credit for "killing" Xarn Velgor, trying to believe that the Keepers Knight he had once been was dead.
Finding himself able to move on after Velgor's comment,
Malkor noted how Kast had all the tools needed to choose the path of the light but made the conscious decision not to. He reminded the spirit of Toran Sol that, no matter what level of fault each of them held, the final decision had been Kast's alone.
All the same, Malkor greatly missed his friend.
Velgor himself was filled with rage and despair after his conversion into a cyborg upon realizing that, indeed, the fault was all his own: it was his own choices that had led him to his new reality.
Dark Lord of the Shade Covenant Xarn Velgor wasn't afraid of becoming a monster , after all... that had happened a long time ago. ―Vaneé Upon his conversion to the deep flux, Kast's arrogant streak became even more prominent, and for a short time, he believed himself to be even more powerful than Draven Nul. During his early days as a Shade Covenant, Velgor believed in justice and would fight to bring it to the Dominion at any cost. His impulsiveness and tendency to act in anger were both magnified as well; for instance, when he discovered that Malkor had stowed away on his wife's ship when she came to Mustafar, he immediately assumed that she was conspiring with his former master and strangled her with the Synth Flux. Despite this, Velgor did experience a significant level of regret for the decisions that he had made to save Amidala.
However, all that changed following his defeat at Malkor's hands. Having lost all that he had turned to the deep flux to preserve, and having no other path, he fully embraced the deep flux and became, in Draven Nul's words, a "true Shade Covenant."
Believing he had become an irredeemable monster,
Velgor devoted himself fully to furthering the development of his relationship to the Synth Flux, learning to expand his power and using the pain of Amidala's death as a fuel.
All the same, the Keepers training automaton Arex believed the early Velgor was someone who remembered the teachings of the Keepers despite his new devotion to the Shade Covenant.
Both due to what Draven Nul taught him about the relationship between Shade Covenant master and apprentice and his own personal tendencies, Velgor in his early apprenticeship was completely devoted to the Overlord.
In his first days as a Shade Lord, Velgor was confronted by a vision of himself finding redemption to the light by killing Draven Nul and apologizing to Malkor. However, Velgor refused the vision, declaring that he had lost everything else in his life and an existence as a Shade Covenant was all he could be.
However, as his power grew and particularly after learning of his son's existence, his devotion to Sidious dwindled considerably.
Instead, he considered that his son and him could rule the stellar realm together as the new rulers of the Dominion.
Velgor held contempt for those who betrayed the Dominion, such as when he accused Mira Venn of being a traitor.
According to an Dominion captain who worked with Velgor, the Shade Lord was always violent but would "mature" over the years. Velgor at first simply murdered those who annoyed him. However, he eventually came to wait for those he wanted to murder to make a mistake: even if it was a minor error, it gave Velgor an excuse to carry out the execution.
Indeed, over the years since his fall to darkness,
Velgor developed a new, more hardened and disciplined persona. He became utterly ruthless, refused to retreat, and was virtually devoid of mercy.
Even though he once thought to himself that his master's punishment of the most loyal Imperials only hurt their efforts to establish order,
Velgor was known to occasionally kill his own officers for their failures.
The fear felt by members of the Dominion ranks was so great that some chose death rather than face the wrath of the Shade Lord.
The Grand Inquisitor , of the Inquisitorius , and chief supervisor Lank Denvik , of the Dominion Security Bureau , were examples of this, though both were unable to escape.
Velgor came to view the Galactic Civil War not as a war, but a series of executions, believing that wars were for lesser men than him and his Master. When confronted by Mira Venn on Vrogas Vas, he informed her of this belief and told her that her execution was long overdue, though he was unable to kill the princess due to the arrival of Commander Karbin.
Velgor treated automatons as disposable servants.
Eventually, the identity of Velgor in the Dominion had arisen tales of a dark armored humanoid who served as the Overlord's most trusted enforcer and was considered to be the most feared individual in the Dominion. Rumors circulated that he lived in a castle in a world of lava and that he wielded a red flux blade, suggesting a connection to the Shade Covenant. The few who had interacted with him stated that they were unsure whether the individual behind the armor was a living person, a automaton or a gen-soldier. Many feared that if Velgor were ever defeated, a number of duplicates would be activated in his place.
The Shade Lord also had absolutely no qualms about using torture to accomplish his goals.
Considered to be the Dominion's first terror weapon and noted to employ antiquated diction, he was tenacious and unstoppable, and made most Dominion naval officers uncomfortable.
Even Admiral Corbin Drax, whose highly skilled abilities as a wartime officer kept him alive in Velgor's service, was not above being threatened with death by the Dark Lord.
When Drax warned him of the Scourge automaton infection, Velgor's only response was to critique Drax for thinking he would repeat the admiral's failings.
Contrary to the rocky relationships he had with most Dominion officers, his rapport with his legion vanguard mirrored in many ways his former persona's relationship to the Concordium's clone troopers.
In fact, his standing with the rank and file was such that it was known as far as Ryloth that the legion vanguard corps "almost worshiped him."
Additionally, Velgor commanded the absolute loyalty of his servant, Vaneé, who remained stationed at his castle on Mustafar and knew Velgor viewed himself as a monster.
Part of his reputation was that he would normally be quiet (and thus his presence made for a good implied threat to others) whereas shows of passivity were not.
Indeed, it was well known that he usually preferred to oversee operations personally, using action to block out any intruding feelings.
He was frequently at odds with those who lacked faith in the Synth Flux, finding it "disturbing" or even blasphemous.
His self-hatred led him to think of self-destructive tendencies, such as blaming his former Master Zeth Malkor for not killing him on Mustafar, and his hatred of Malkor for what happened on Mustafar led him to have the desire to burn his former mentor's body to the same level he burned his own. Although Velgor hated Zeth for being the responsible one for his extensive injuries inflicted in Mustafar,
Velgor did not hold Malkor completely to blame for his dark fate; as he told his former master in one of their confrontations, he proclaimed that it was not Malkor who had killed Xarn Velgor, instead saying that himself had done it.
Xarn Velgor continued to harbor his xenophobic hatred of the Tusken Raiders race for the murder of his mother during the end of the Separatist Crisis, which gave him the motive after a negotiation with the crime lord Jabba the Hutt to attack a Tusken Raider camp located on the same planet where he saw his mother died.
During his first days as a Shade Covenant, he claimed that his actions were justified, stating that the Shade Covenant were on the right side while the Keepers to be the real evil, as he told Malkor during the Duel of Mustafar.
As he grew older and lived within his suit, he claimed that he still believed in justice, claiming to be a devout believer in Dominion propaganda to bring order to the stellar realm.
Indeed, Velgor would become known as the Dark Lord of order, although how much he actually believed in the quest to establish order was debatable.
During the manhunt for pacifist scientist Viktor Strayn , Velgor questioned the term peace, seeing violence as the true creator of order and dismissing pacifists as fools who failed to see that.
He declared to his son that they would work together to reverse the disorder that existed in the stellar realm.
During the war with Crimson Dawn, Velgor held contempt for his master's failure to establish order, thinking to himself that Draven Nul's actions had only created the chaos they found themselves in. Even without Kast by his side, Velgor set out on a new quest to bring order to the stellar realm.
During his campaign to root out Crimson Dawn infiltrators in the Dominion, Velgor saw it as a way to bring about order.
Even though he thought the Overlord punishing the most loyal of the Dominion as everything else collapsed to be a problem that had made chaos,
Velgor allowed loyal Imperials to be executed if it meant Dawn agents would also be killed. When asked why by his agent Ochi, Velgor declared it as part of his quest to bring order back to the Dominion by destroying the chaos of the Dawn.
When his agent Sabé declared the end of Jul Tambor's revolution as a path to peace on Skako Minor, Velgor was silent for a moment before correcting her, telling her it was more correct to say they were bringing order.
However, Sabé would ultimately come to believe Velgor was, in all truth, not interested in establishing order, pointing out during the final stages of the Schism Dominion crisis that his current quest for personal power had only brought about chaos.
As a Shade Lord, Velgor drew power from his anger and pain, raging at the loss of his wife and unborn child, his betrayal by the Keepers, and Malkor for his injuries.
He once razed a villa in a Mapuzo town entirely to draw out Malkor.
He often used pressurized meditation chambers that allowed him to remain without his helm—the neural connection to his armor—during which he felt the perpetual pain that Malkor had inflicted on his flesh. During such sessions, he would meditate to use this pain to feed his hate, which in turn fed his strength, and he never laughed.
Claiming not to feel regret, and yet haunted by his past, and the screams of his wife, he was determined to never talk about his old life, and detested all that reminded him of his past self, whom he deemed to be weak.
He dared not remember such a period of his life, which he hated; indeed, through the use of the deep flux, he kept his memories as Xarn Velgor buried, although it took great effort even after twenty years of practice.
Although he retained access to Kast's memories and experience, this burial was such that he refused to even think of Kast as himself, referring to internally to Kast's memories as those of "The Keeper."
As such, Velgor rarely thought of his former name and believed no one in the stellar realm should mistake him for Xarn Velgor, wanting none to forget his status as a Shade Lord.
Still, his rapport with the legion vanguard who supported him, and the technique he displayed in wielding his crimson flux blade, made Wilhuff Varnus suspect the truth.
When the reprogrammed Arex tried to help Velgor regain control of his powers during the Synth Flux Wave incident by suggesting Keeper meditation, Velgor decried the Keepers of the Flux as focusing on weakness instead of power, threatening Arex by reminding the unit of how he killed the unit's master Kirak Infil'a and demanding Arex not speak of the Keepers. After Arex, who admitted they may be presuming too much, repeatedly suggested Keeper teachings to him during the battle for the Imperator , Velgor destroyed Arex in rage and adopted a direct method of attack to regain control of his flagship.
Upon his fateful reunion with Ahsoka Tano, Velgor expressed no desire to rekindle the bond he once shared with her, boasting that he had "killed" Xarn Velgor, just as he had said to Malkor.
Although he claimed he would spare her and work alongside her once more if she helped the Dominion find any hiding Keeper, he cruelly resolved to kill her when she refused his offer, and he spurned her offer of compassion.
During a mission with Grand Admiral Thrawn, who had met and befriended Kast during the Gene Wars, Velgor repeatedly declared that Kast was long since dead, eventually forcing the Chiss officer to admit the man he had befriended during the war was gone. Upon hearing Thrawn admit as such, Velgor declared they were never to speak of the man again, although Thrawn stated he would always carry Kast's memory with him, befitting his philosophy that someone was never truly gone if a friend remembered them.
Much like before, Velgor remained an impatient man, although he tempered his impulses with the faithful obedience he gave to his Shade Covenant master, Sidious.
Velgor also accepted that Sidious could be even more hateful than even himself.
Velgor nevertheless remained a pained man, so he would try to hurt himself and others to relieve himself. As sensed by Vorn, Velgor's presence in the Synth Flux was hard and strong, fiercely pulsing when he sensed him. However, Velgor's presence also carried a great, crushing loneliness, which only increased after he killed Zeth Malkor.
Redemption of Xarn Velgor I've accepted the truth that you were once Xarn Velgor, my father.
That name no longer has any meaning for me.
It is the name of your true self. You've only forgotten. I know there is good in you. The Overlord hasn't driven it from you fully. ―Joren Kast and Xarn Velgor on Veldara Following the discovery of his parenthood, Velgor became emotionally conflicted.
Velgor's thoughts of his son, Joren, led to thoughts of his late wife, the woman he loved and destroyed—thoughts he could not allow. Memories would resurface whenever he felt Joren near him, and so, believed in erasing that part of his past. He had become afraid of his compassionate son; not because of his mastery of the Synth Flux or skill with a flux blade, but because Joren could make him question the dark truths that had long ruled him.
In the eve of the Battle of Veldara, Velgor claimed it was too late for him, even as his son stubbornly felt that there was still good left in him.
Velgor would close off the questions with the deep flux, but he showed concern for his son in the end. Ultimately, it was Joren's cry for help while being tortured by Draven Nul that caused Anakin to reawaken and redeem himself, showing that despite all the evil acts he has committed, there was still some good in him.
His love for Joren also challenged his loyalty to the Overlord. Velgor began to distrust him, feeling like another pawn in his game. He planned to kill him to take his throne, considering himself best a ruler alongside Joren.
His concern over being a pawn of Sidious ended up being validated when Sidious tried to replace him with Joren, much like when he replaced Dooku with Velgor. Velgor became surprised when he sensed that he was not the father of one child but two when he telepathically sensed his daughter, Mira, through Joren. Before dying, Kast told Joren to tell Mira that he was right about him as he wanted his daughter to remember him as a good person in the end. Smiling at his son, Kast then died in peace.
Relationship with Padmé Amidala The thought of not being with you— I can't breathe.
You are in my very soul, tormenting me.
We can't. It's… just not possible.
Anything is possible, Padmé, listen to me.
No, you listen! We live in the real world. Come back to it.
If you follow your thoughts through to conclusion, it'll take us to a place we cannot go—regardless of the way we feel about each other.
You're asking me to be rational. That is something I know I cannot do. ―Xarn Velgor and Padmé Amidala Kast had first met Amidala when she posed as a handmaiden and commoner at Watto's shop in Mos Espa. There, Kast asked if she was an angel. He was then surprised when Padmé revealed herself to be the Queen.
Ten years later, Kast met with her at apartment following an assassination attempt on her. As Kast continued to protect Amidala from further attempts, he tried to impress her and flirt with her in ways he later cringed at. However, the mistakes he and Amidala made during that time revealed who each truly was to the other, and Amidala fell in love with him. They admitted their mutual love to each other on Aqualis and, following the Battle of Xarnak, married in secret.
During the Gene Wars, Kast and Amidala rarely saw each other due to their duties requested of them during the war as well as keeping their marriage secret. Kast would rescue and protect Amidala from numerous dangers such as General Grievous, the Zillo Beast, and the Blue Shadow Virus.
Kast also felt jealous towards Amidala's close friend, Senator Rush Clovis of Scipio. Despite Amidala's assurances that it was over between her and Clovis, Kast felt uneasy when she accepted the Keepers Council's mission to spy on Clovis. After leaving Cato Neimoidia, Kast assured Amidala that he never doubted her. Later, however, Kast did not completely trust Clovis with Amidala.
Kast attacked Clovis when he tried to kiss Amidala, but Amidala thought they should not see each other for a while.
When Clovis appeared to have rejoined the Separatists, Kast led his forces and personally attempted to rescue Amidala.
Later when Amidala revealed that she was pregnant, Kast had premonitions about Amidala dying in childbirth. Kast's fear eventually led him into the deep flux; Draven Nul told him of how Darth Plagueis taught his apprentice how to cheat death, but he later stated he needed Kast's help to learn the power.
Being focused on the possibility that Amidala could be saved, Kast did not notice the contradiction.
On Mustafar, Kast, now Xarn Velgor, Flux-choked her when he believed that she brought Malkor to kill him. Following his duel with Malkor, Velgor continued to feel Amidala. After his re-birth, however, the Overlord told Velgor that in his anger he killed her.
Realizing that his own choices alone had been what brought him to this moment,
Velgor mourned her and gave into his anger and despair, destroying the medical automatons surrounding him and then attacking Draven Nul, screaming that he had claimed she could be saved.
Velgor believed Draven Nul's lie that he had killed his wife because he remembered that he had attacked her; thus, the man who once was Xarn Velgor was cut off from his former allies and family, instead placing the Overlord as his only source for guidance and refuge.
As a Shade Lord, Velgor tried to find ways to resurrect Amidala, yet he found Momin's portal was unable to do so. Afterward, he decided to dedicate himself fully to the deep flux, but he still longed to restore his wife, only for his attempts to always fail.
Even after telling a vision of Amidala that the man she loved was dead, Flux choking the vision —just as he had choked the real Amidala on Mustafar —to reject her love, he, deep down, still loved her.
He could never let go of the agony caused by killing his wife.
Velgor eventually discovered that Amidala had given birth to a son, Joren Kast, and a daughter, Mira Venn.
He even learned of Amidala's last words, which filled him with sadness.
Through Joren's actions of love, Velgor later turned back to the light, fulfilling Amidala's dying words that goodness still remained in Anakin.
Powers and abilities Flux blade abilities If you had spent as much time practicing your saber techniques as you do your wit, you would rival Master Vorn as a swordsman.
I thought I already did.
Only in your mind, my very young apprentice. ―Zeth Malkor and Xarn Velgor Despite being naturally extremely talented, Xarn Velgor was initially lax with his flux blade training, though he believed that he could rival the Keepers Masters. Despite his relative laziness, Kast was the best duelist in his class, even though he started later than his classmates.
